## Canada Transportation Act {#canada-transportation-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 1996, c. 10
:::

Assented to 1996-05-29

An Act to continue the National Transportation Agency as the Canadian
Transportation Agency, to consolidate and revise the National
Transportation Act, 1987 and the Railway Act and to amend or repeal
other Acts as a consequence
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: Enacts
Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate and House
of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54105 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canada Transportation Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4).

## [Her Majesty]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54108 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on Her Majesty

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** This Act is binding on Her
Majesty in right of Canada or a province.

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54111 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application generally

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** This Act applies in respect
of transportation matters under the legislative authority of Parliament.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflicts

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (2), where there is a conflict between any order or
  regulation made under this Act in respect of a particular mode of
  transportation and any rule, order or regulation made under any other
  Act of Parliament in respect of that particular mode of
  transportation, the order or regulation made under this Act prevails.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), nothing in or done under
  the authority of this Act, other than Division IV of Part III, affects
  the operation of the [Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}International agreements respecting air
  services

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency or conflict between
  an international agreement or convention respecting air services to
  which Canada is a party and the [Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34), the
  provisions of the agreement or convention prevail to the extent of the
  inconsistency or conflict.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 4
- 2007, c. 19, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

## [National Transportation Policy]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54123 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** It is declared that a
competitive, economic and efficient national transportation system that
meets the highest practicable safety and security standards and
contributes to a sustainable environment and makes the best use of all
modes of transportation at the lowest total cost is essential to serve
the needs of its users, advance the well-being of Canadians and enable
competitiveness and economic growth in both urban and rural areas
throughout Canada. Those objectives are most likely to be achieved when

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} competition and market forces, both within and among
  the various modes of transportation, are the prime agents in providing
  viable and effective transportation services;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} regulation and strategic public intervention are used
  to achieve economic, safety, security, environmental or social
  outcomes that cannot be achieved satisfactorily by competition and
  market forces and do not unduly favour, or reduce the inherent
  advantages of, any particular mode of transportation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} rates and conditions do not constitute an undue
  obstacle to the movement of traffic within Canada or to the export of
  goods from Canada;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the transportation system is accessible without undue
  obstacle to the mobility of all persons;

- [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} the transportation system is accessible without
  barriers to persons with disabilities; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} governments and the private sector work together for
  an integrated transportation system.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 5
- 2007, c. 19, s. 2
- [2019, c. 10, s. 166](#1154735-1191853){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154735-1191853.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54133 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** In this Act,

[[Agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Transportation
    Agency continued by subsection 7(1); ([Office]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is engaged in the
    transport of goods or passengers by any means of transport under the
    legislative authority of
    Parliament; ([transporteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chairperson of the
    Agency; ([président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[class 1 rail carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[class 1 rail carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian National Railway Company,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian Pacific Railway Company,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} BNSF Railway Company,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} CSX Transportation, Inc.,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} Norfolk Southern Railway Company,

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} Union Pacific Railroad Company, and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any [[railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
      defined in section 87, that is specified in the
      regulations; ([transporteur ferroviaire de catégorie
      1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes rolling stock and
    mail; ([marchandises]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a member of the Agency
    appointed under subsection 7(2) and includes a temporary
    member; ([membre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Transport; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[radioactive material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[radioactive material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
    in subsection 1(1) of the [Packaging and Transport of Nuclear
    Substances Regulations, 2015](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-145). It
    includes a dangerous good with any of UN numbers 2908 to 2913, 2915
    to 2917, 2919, 2977, 2978, 3321 to 3333 and 3507 that are set out in
    Column 1 of the Dangerous Goods List in Chapter 3.2 of the
    Recommendations on the Transport of Dangerous Goods --- Model
    Regulations, Eighteenth revised edition, 2013, published by the
    United Nations; ([matière radioactive]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[rolling stock]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[rolling stock]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a locomotive, engine,
    motor car, tender, snow-plough, flanger and any car or railway
    equipment that is designed for movement on its wheels on the rails
    of a railway; ([matériel roulant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[shipper]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[shipper]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who sends or receives
    goods by means of a carrier or intends to do
    so; ([expéditeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sitting day of Parliament]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sitting day of Parliament]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a day on
    which either House of Parliament sits; ([jour de
    séance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[superior court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[superior court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in Ontario, the Superior Court of Justice,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in Quebec, the Superior Court,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in New Brunswick, Manitoba, Saskatchewan and
      Alberta, the Court of Queen's Bench,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in Nova Scotia, British Columbia, Prince Edward
      Island, Yukon and the Northwest Territories, the Supreme Court,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in Newfoundland and Labrador, the Trial Division
      of the Supreme Court, and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in Nunavut, the Nunavut Court of Justice; ([cour
      supérieure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[temporary member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[temporary member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a temporary member of
    the Agency appointed under subsection 9(1); ([membre
    temporaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[TIH (Toxic Inhalation Hazard) material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[TIH (Toxic Inhalation Hazard) material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    a gas or substance that is included in Class 2.3 of the
    [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286) or that, under paragraph
    2.28(c) of those Regulations, is included in Class 6.1 of those
    Regulations. It includes a dangerous good with a UN number that is
    set out in Column 1 of the Dangerous Goods List in Chapter 3.2 of
    the Recommendations on the Transport of Dangerous Goods -- Model
    Regulations, Eighteenth revised edition, 2013, published by the
    United Nations and that is listed in Schedule III; ([matière toxique
    par inhalation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Vice-Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Vice-Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Vice-Chairperson
    of the Agency. ([vice-président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 6
- 1998, c. 30, ss. 13(F), 15(E)
- 1999, c. 3, s. 20
- 2002, c. 7, s. 114(E)
- 2015, c. 3, s. 29, c. 31, s. 2
- 2018, c. 10, s. 2
:::

[Previous
Version](section-6-20160618.html "Link to previous version of section 6"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Power of the Governor in Council]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54166 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council

**[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations specifying railway companies for the purpose of
paragraph (g) of the definition [[class 1 rail
carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated person

**[[6.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.11 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, by regulations, designate a person for the purposes of
subsections 50(1.001) and (3) and 51(1), (3) and (4).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 436](#1410583-1414023){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410583-1414023.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [Electronic Administration and Enforcement]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1171098 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic means

- **[[6.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister administers or enforces an Act of Parliament, the
  Minister may do so using electronic means.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, any person or class of persons
  who are designated by the Minister under an Act of Parliament for the
  purposes of the administration and enforcement of that Act may, in the
  exercise of their powers or the performance of their duties and
  functions, use the electronic means that are made available or
  specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a person who has been
  authorized by the Minister to do anything that may be done by the
  Minister under an Act of Parliament, may do so using the electronic
  means that are made available or specified by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 214](#1156278-1171107){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156278-1171107.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information

**[[6.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.3 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 6.4 and 6.5, providing information includes

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} making an application, request or decision;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} giving notice; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} submitting a document.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 214](#1156278-1171114){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156278-1171114.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for electronic version

**[[6.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.4 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of an
Act of Parliament that the Minister administers or enforces, a
requirement under that Act to provide a signature or to provide
information in a paper-based format is met if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an electronic version of the signature or information
  is provided by electronic means that are made available or specified
  by the Minister; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other condition that is provided for in
  regulations made under paragraph 6.5(a) is met.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 214](#1156278-1171120){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156278-1171120.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[6.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.5 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the application of sections 6.2 and 6.4,
  including the technology or format to be used, or the standards,
  specifications or processes to be followed, including for the making
  or verifying of an electronic signature and the manner in which it is
  to be used;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} setting out the circumstances in which a person who
  must provide a signature or information under an Act that the Minister
  administers or enforces is required to do so using electronic means
  and specifying those means;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the Minister to permit or direct the use
  of electronic means, other than those referred to in paragraph (b), or
  non-electronic means and setting out the circumstances in which the
  Minister may do so; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the date and time when, and the place
  where, the electronic version of information is deemed to be sent or
  received.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 214](#1156278-1171128){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156278-1171128.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [Exemptions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1250582 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[6.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may, in the form and manner specified by the Minister, apply to
  the Minister for an order that exempts any person or thing, or class
  of persons or things, from the application of any provision of an Act
  of Parliament that the Minister administers or enforces or any
  provision of an instrument made under that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on receiving the application,
  require the provision of any information that is necessary for the
  Minister to process and assess the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts to Her Majesty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may refuse to process or assess the
  application if the applicant has not paid an amount that, under an Act
  of Parliament that the Minister administers or enforces, constitutes a
  debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 215](#1156280-1250591){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156280-1250591.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[6.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, by order, exempt, for a period of not more than five
  years and subject to any conditions that the Minister considers
  appropriate, any person or thing, or class of persons or things, from
  the application of any provision of an Act of Parliament that the
  Minister administers or enforces or any provision of an instrument
  made under that Act, if the Minister is of the opinion that the
  exemption, having regard to the purposes of that Act, is in the public
  interest and that the exemption promotes innovation in transportation
  through research, development or testing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, extend the period of an
  exemption once, for a further period of not more than five years,
  subject to any conditions that the Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply to an order made under this section that applies to a single
  person or thing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall ensure that an order referred to
  in subsection (3) is accessible to the public unless the Minister is
  of the opinion that it would be inappropriate for reasons that include
  safety or security considerations or the protection of confidential or
  personal information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions under other Act

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the making of an order under
  subsection (1) does not preclude or limit the exercise of a power to
  exempt under an Act of Parliament that is administered or enforced by
  the Minister and vice versa.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 215](#1156280-1250604){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156280-1250604.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cost recovery

**[[6.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.8 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may
recover any costs associated with the processing and assessing of an
application under section 6.6 and may refuse to make the order requested
until those costs are recovered from the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 215](#1156280-1250608){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156280-1250608.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Administration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-54171 .Part}

### [Canadian Transportation Agency]{.HTitleText2} {#h-54172 .Subheading}

#### [Continuation and Organization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54173 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency continued

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  agency known as the National Transportation Agency is continued as the
  Canadian Transportation Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of Agency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall consist of not more than five
  members appointed by the Governor in Council, and such temporary
  members as are appointed under subsection 9(1), each of whom must, on
  appointment or reappointment and while serving as a member, be a
  Canadian citizen or a permanent resident within the meaning of
  subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall designate one of the
  members appointed under subsection (2) to be the Chairperson of the
  Agency and one of the other members appointed under that subsection to
  be the Vice-Chairperson of the Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 7
- 2001, c. 27, s. 221
- 2007, c. 19, s. 3
- 2015, c. 3, s. 30(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of members

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  member appointed under subsection 7(2) shall hold office during good
  behaviour for a term of not more than five years and may be removed
  for cause by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member appointed under subsection 7(2) is eligible
  to be reappointed on the expiration of a first or subsequent term of
  office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation in office

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a member appointed under subsection 7(2) ceases to
  hold office, the Chairperson may authorize the member to continue to
  hear any matter that was before the member on the expiry of the
  member's term of office and that member is deemed to be a member of
  the Agency, but that person's status as a member does not preclude the
  appointment of up to five members under subsection 7(2) or up to three
  temporary members under subsection 9(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 8
- 2007, c. 19, s. 4
- 2015, c. 3, s. 31(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary members

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may appoint temporary members of the Agency from the roster
  of individuals established by the Governor in Council under subsection
  (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Roster

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint any individual to
  a roster of candidates for the purpose of subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum number

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Not more than three temporary members shall hold
  office at any one time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of temporary members

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A temporary member shall hold office during good
  behaviour for a term of not more than one year and may be removed for
  cause by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No reappointment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person who has served two consecutive terms as a
  temporary member is not, during the twelve months following the
  completion of the person's second term, eligible to be reappointed to
  the Agency as a temporary member.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members --- conflicts of interest

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member appointed under subsection 7(2) shall not, directly or
  indirectly, as owner, shareholder, director, officer, partner or
  otherwise,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be engaged in a transportation undertaking or
    business; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have an interest in a transportation undertaking or
    business or an interest in the manufacture or distribution of
    transportation plant or equipment, unless the distribution is merely
    incidental to the general merchandising of goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary members may not hold other
  office

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} During the term of office of a temporary member, the
  member shall not accept or hold any office or employment that is
  inconsistent with the member's duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of conflict of interest

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an interest referred to in subsection (1) vests in
  a member appointed under subsection 7(2) for the benefit of the member
  by will or succession, the member shall, within three months after the
  vesting, absolutely dispose of the interest.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 10
- 2015, c. 3, s. 32(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Remuneration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54214 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member shall be paid such remuneration and allowances as may be fixed
  by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each member is entitled to be paid reasonable travel
  and living expenses incurred by the member in carrying out duties
  under this Act or any other Act of Parliament while absent from the
  member's ordinary place of work.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members --- retirement pensions

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member appointed under subsection 7(2) is deemed to be employed in the
  public service for the purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary members not included

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A temporary member is deemed not to be employed in
  the public service for the purposes of the [Public Service
  Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36) unless the Governor in Council, by
  order, deems the member to be so employed for those purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accident compensation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the [Government Employees
  Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and any regulation made pursuant to
  section 9 of the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2), a member is deemed
  to be an employee in the federal public administration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 12
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 224(E), 225(E)
- 2015, c. 3, s. 33(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Chairperson]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54229 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of Chairperson

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson is the
chief executive officer of the Agency and has the supervision over and
direction of the work of the members and its staff, including the
apportionment of work among the members and the assignment of members to
deal with any matter before the Agency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of Chairperson

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** In the event of the
absence or incapacity of the Chairperson or if the office of Chairperson
is vacant, the Vice-Chairperson has all the powers and shall perform all
the duties and functions of the Chairperson.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of both Chairperson and
Vice-Chairperson

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson may
authorize one or more of the members to act as Chairperson for the time
being if both the Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson are absent or unable
to act.

#### [Quorum]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54236 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the Agency's rules, two
  members constitute a quorum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum lost because of incapacity of
  member

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a member who is conducting a hearing in respect
  of a matter becomes incapacitated or dies during the hearing or after
  the conclusion of the hearing but before rendering a decision and
  quorum is lost as a result, the Chairperson may, with the consent of
  all the parties to the hearing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the incapacity or death occurs during the
    hearing, authorize another member to continue the hearing and render
    a decision, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the incapacity or death occurs after the
    conclusion of the hearing, authorize another member to examine the
    evidence presented at the hearing and render a decision,

  and in either case, the quorum in respect of the matter is deemed
  never to have been lost.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum not lost because of incapacity
  of member

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a member who is conducting a hearing in respect
  of a matter becomes incapacitated or dies during the hearing and
  quorum is not lost as a result, another member may be assigned by the
  Chairperson to participate in the hearing and in the rendering of a
  decision.

#### [Rules]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54247 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may make rules
respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sittings of the Agency and the carrying on of its
  work;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner of and procedures for dealing with matters
  and business before the Agency, including the circumstances in which
  hearings may be held in private; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the number of members that are required to hear any
  matter or perform any of the functions of the Agency under this Act or
  any other Act of Parliament.

#### [Head Office]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54253 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  head office of the Agency shall be in the National Capital Region
  described in the schedule to the [National Capital
  Act](/eng/acts/N-4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residence of members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members appointed under subsection 7(2) shall
  reside in the National Capital Region described in the schedule to the
  [National Capital Act](/eng/acts/N-4) or within any distance of it
  that the Governor in Council determines.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 18
- 2007, c. 19, s. 5
- 2008, c. 21, s. 61
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 18"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Staff]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54261 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secretary, officers and employees

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** The Secretary of the
Agency and the other officers and employees that are necessary for the
proper conduct of the business of the Agency shall be appointed in
accordance with the [Public Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technical experts

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may appoint
and, subject to any applicable Treasury Board directive, fix the
remuneration of experts or persons who have technical or special
knowledge to assist the Agency in an advisory capacity in respect of any
matter before the Agency.

#### [Records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54266 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of Secretary

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Secretary of the Agency shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} maintain a record in which shall be entered a true
    copy of every rule, order, decision and regulation of the Agency and
    any other documents that the Agency requires to be entered in it;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep at the Agency's office a copy of all rules,
    orders, decisions and regulations of the Agency and the records of
    proceedings of the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entries in record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The entry of a document in the record referred to in
  paragraph (1)(a) shall constitute the original record of the document.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of documents obtainable

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** On the application of any
person, and on payment of a fee fixed by the Agency, the Secretary of
the Agency or, in the absence of the Secretary, the person assigned by
the Chairperson to act in the absence shall issue under the seal of the
Agency to the applicant a certified copy of any rule, order, regulation
or any other document that has been issued by the Agency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial notice of documents

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Judicial notice shall be taken of a
  document issued by the Agency under its seal without proof of the
  signature or official character of the person appearing to have signed
  it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of deposited documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to be certified by the
  Secretary of the Agency as being a true copy of a document deposited
  or filed with or approved by the Agency, or any portion of such a
  document, is evidence that the document is so deposited, filed or
  approved and, if stated in the certificate, of the time when the
  document was deposited, filed or approved.

#### [Powers of Agency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54281 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Policy governs Agency

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** The powers, duties and
functions of the Agency respecting any matter that comes within its
jurisdiction under an Act of Parliament shall be exercised and performed
in conformity with any policy direction issued to the Agency under
section 43.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency powers in general

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** The Agency has, with
respect to all matters necessary or proper for the exercise of its
jurisdiction, the attendance and examination of witnesses, the
production and inspection of documents, the enforcement of its orders or
regulations and the entry on and inspection of property, all the powers,
rights and privileges that are vested in a superior court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to award costs

- **[[25.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4),
  the Agency has all the powers that the Federal Court has to award
  costs in any proceeding before it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs may be fixed or taxed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Costs may be fixed in any case at a sum certain or
  may be taxed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may direct by whom and to whom costs are
  to be paid and by whom they are to be taxed and allowed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scale

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may make rules specifying a scale under
  which costs are to be taxed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compelling observance of obligations

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may require a
person to do or refrain from doing any thing that the person is or may
be required to do or is prohibited from doing under any Act of
Parliament that is administered in whole or in part by the Agency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may grant the whole or part of an application, or may make any
  order or grant any further or other relief that the Agency considers
  appropriate.

- [(2) and (3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2008, c. 5, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, on terms or otherwise, make or allow
  any amendments in any proceedings before it.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2008, c. 5, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 27
- 2008, c. 5, s. 1
- 2018, c. 10, s. 4(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-27-20080228.html "Link to previous version of section 27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may in any order direct that the order or a portion or
  provision of it shall come into force

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at a future time,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on the happening of any contingency, event or
    condition specified in the order, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on the performance, to the satisfaction of the
    Agency or a person named by it, of any terms that the Agency may
    impose on an interested party,

  and the Agency may direct that the whole or any portion of the order
  shall have force for a limited time or until the happening of a
  specified event.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim orders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, instead of making an order final in
  the first instance, make an interim order and reserve further
  directions either for an adjourned hearing of the matter or for
  further application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for making decisions

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency shall make its decision in any proceedings before it as
  expeditiously as possible, but no later than one hundred and twenty
  days after the originating documents are received, unless the parties
  agree to an extension or this Act or a regulation made under
  subsection (2) provides otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for specified classes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by regulation, prescribe
  periods of less than one hundred and twenty days within which the
  Agency shall make its decision in respect of such classes of
  proceedings as are specified in the regulation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pending proceedings

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** The fact that a suit,
prosecution or proceeding involving a question of fact is pending in any
court does not deprive the Agency of jurisdiction to hear and determine
the same question of fact.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fact finding is conclusive

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** The finding or
determination of the Agency on a question of fact within its
jurisdiction is binding and conclusive.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of decisions and orders

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may review,
rescind or vary any decision or order made by it or may re-hear any
application before deciding it if, in the opinion of the Agency, since
the decision or order or the hearing of the application, there has been
a change in the facts or circumstances pertaining to the decision, order
or hearing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement of decision or order

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  decision or order of the Agency may be made an order of the Federal
  Court or of any superior court and is enforceable in the same manner
  as such an order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} To make a decision or order an order of a court,
  either the usual practice and procedure of the court in such matters
  may be followed or the Secretary of the Agency may file with the
  registrar of the court a certified copy of the decision or order,
  signed by the Chairperson and sealed with the Agency's seal, at which
  time the decision or order becomes an order of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of variation or rescission

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a decision or order that has been made an order
  of a court is rescinded or varied by a subsequent decision or order of
  the Agency, the order of the court is deemed to have been cancelled
  and the subsequent decision or order may be made an order of the
  court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Option to enforce

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, before or after one of its decisions
  or orders is made an order of a court, enforce the decision or order
  by its own action.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 33
- 2002, c. 8, s. 122
- 2006, c. 11, s. 17
- 2007, c. 19, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20061214.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees and charges

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may, after consulting with the Minister, make rules respecting
  the fees and charges to be paid in relation to the administration or
  enforcement of any provision of this Act or the regulations whose
  administration or enforcement is the responsibility of the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making a rule under subsection (1), the Agency
  shall consult with any persons or organizations that the Agency
  considers to be interested in the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to His Majesty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Fees or charges required to be paid under this
  section constitute a debt to His Majesty in right of Canada and may be
  recovered as such in a court of competent jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 34
- [2023, c. 26, s. 452](#1410641-1414111){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410641-1414111.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees for witnesses

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** Every person summoned to
attend before the Agency under this Part or before a person making an
inquiry under this Part shall receive the fees and allowances for so
doing that the Agency may, by regulation, prescribe.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of regulations required

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  regulation made by the Agency under this Act must be made with the
  approval of the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advance notice of regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall give the Minister notice of every
  regulation proposed to be made by the Agency under this Act.

#### [Mediation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54349 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request by parties

- **[[36.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  there is a dispute concerning a matter within the Agency's
  jurisdiction, all the parties to the dispute may, by agreement, make a
  request to the Agency for mediation. On receipt of the request, the
  Agency shall refer the dispute for mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of mediator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When a dispute is referred for mediation, the
  Chairperson shall appoint one or two persons to mediate the dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediator not to act in other
  proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who is appointed to mediate a dispute may
  not act in any other proceedings before the Agency in relation to that
  matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of mediation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All matters relating to the mediation of a dispute
  shall be kept confidential, unless the parties to the dispute
  otherwise agree, and information provided by a party for the purposes
  of mediation shall not be used for any other purpose without the
  consent of that party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for completion of mediation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Unless the parties to a dispute otherwise agree, the
  mediation of the dispute shall be completed within 30 days after the
  dispute is referred for mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of mediation on proceedings

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The mediation has the effect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} staying for the period of the mediation any
    proceedings before the Agency in so far as they relate to a matter
    that is the subject of the mediation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} extending the time within which the Agency may make
    a decision or determination under this Act with regard to those
    proceedings by the period of the mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of mediation agreement

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An agreement that is reached as a result of mediation
  may be filed with the Agency and, after filing, is enforceable as if
  it were an order of the Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 7
:::

#### [Information and Informal Dispute Resolution Services]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54369 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information and guidance

- **[[36.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall take measures to
  inform the public in respect of the provisions of Parts III and IV,
  including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} publishing general information on its Internet
    site; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing information and guidance to any
    interested person on the manner in which remedies under those
    provisions may be accessed, having regard to their particular
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Informal resolution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the Agency or its staff may attempt to
  resolve in an informal manner with a railway company any issue raised
  by an interested person to whom it has provided information and
  guidance. In doing so, the member or staff shall not reveal the
  identity of the interested person without their consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person not to act in proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who exercises the powers or performs the
  duties or functions under paragraph (1)(b) or subsection (2) shall not
  act in any proceedings before the Agency that are related to an issue
  in respect of which the person provided information, guidance or
  informal dispute resolution services.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 5
:::

#### [Mediation or Arbitration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54381 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request by all parties

- **[[36.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  sections 36.1 and 169.1 do not apply, the Agency may mediate or
  arbitrate a dispute relating to any railway matter covered under Part
  III --- other than Division VI.2 --- or Part IV, or to the application
  of any rate or charge for the movement of goods by railways or for the
  provision of incidental services, if requested to do so by all parties
  to the dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of roster

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may establish a roster of persons, which
  may include members and staff of the Agency, to act as mediators or
  arbitrators.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement of costs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The parties are jointly and severally, or solidarily,
  liable to reimburse the Agency its costs arising from the mediation or
  arbitration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediator not to act in other
  proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person who acts as mediator or arbitrator may not
  act in any other proceedings before the Agency in relation to any
  matter that was at issue in the mediation or arbitration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 7
- 2008, c. 5, ss. 8, 9
- 2015, c. 31, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36.2-20080228.html "Link to previous version of section 36.2"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Inquiries]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54393 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry into complaint

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may inquire
into, hear and determine a complaint concerning any act, matter or thing
prohibited, sanctioned or required to be done under any Act of
Parliament that is administered in whole or in part by the Agency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of person to conduct inquiry

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may appoint a member, or an employee of the Agency, to make any
  inquiry that the Agency is authorized to conduct and report to the
  Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dealing with report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the report under subsection (1), the
  Agency may adopt the report as a decision or order of the Agency or
  otherwise deal with it as it considers advisable.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on inquiry

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** A person conducting an
inquiry may, for the purposes of the inquiry,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter and inspect any place, other than a
  dwelling-house, or any structure, work, rolling stock or ship that is
  the property or under the control of any person the entry or
  inspection of which appears to the inquirer to be necessary; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the same powers as are vested in a superior
  court to summon witnesses, enforce their attendance and compel them to
  give evidence and produce any materials, books, papers, plans,
  specifications, drawings and other documents that the inquirer thinks
  necessary.

#### [Review and Appeal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54405 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council may vary or rescind
orders, etc.

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, at any time, in the discretion of the Governor in Council, either
on petition of a party or an interested person or of the Governor in
Council's own motion, vary or rescind any decision, order, rule or
regulation of the Agency, whether the decision or order is made [inter
partes]{.otherLang lang="la"} or otherwise, and whether the rule or
regulation is general or limited in its scope and application, and any
order that the Governor in Council may make to do so is binding on the
Agency and on all parties.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal from Agency

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appeal lies from the Agency to the Federal Court of Appeal on a
  question of law or a question of jurisdiction on leave to appeal being
  obtained from that Court on application made within one month after
  the date of the decision, order, rule or regulation being appealed
  from, or within any further time that a judge of that Court under
  special circumstances allows, and on notice to the parties and the
  Agency, and on hearing those of them that appear and desire to be
  heard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for making appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No appeal, after leave to appeal has been obtained
  under subsection (1), lies unless it is entered in the Federal Court
  of Appeal within sixty days after the order granting leave to appeal
  is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An appeal shall be heard as quickly as is practicable
  and, on the hearing of the appeal, the Court may draw any inferences
  that are not inconsistent with the facts expressly found by the Agency
  and that are necessary for determining the question of law or
  jurisdiction, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency may be heard

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency is entitled to be heard by counsel or
  otherwise on the argument of an appeal.

#### [Report of Agency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54417 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency's report

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  year the Agency shall, before the end of July, make a report on its
  activities for the preceding year and submit it, through the Minister,
  to the Governor in Council describing briefly, in respect of that
  year,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applications to the Agency and the findings on
    them; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the findings of the Agency in regard to any matter
    or thing respecting which the Agency has acted on the request of the
    Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional content

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall include in every report referred to
  in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Agency's assessment of the operation of this
    Act and any difficulties observed in the administration of this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the year to which the report relates,
    information about, including the number of, the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} inspections conducted under this Act for a
      purpose related to verifying compliance or preventing
      non-compliance with any provision of regulations made under
      subsection 170(1) or with any of sections 60 to 62 of the
      [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} orders made under section 181.2,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} orders, in writing, made under section 26 that
      require any person to do or to refrain from doing any thing that
      they are or may be required to do or are prohibited from doing
      under any provision of regulations made under subsection 170(1) or
      any of sections 60 to 62 of the [Accessible Canada
      Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6),

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} notices of violation issued under section 180
      that identify a violation referred to in subsection 177(3), and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} inquiries made under any of sections 172, 172.1
      and 172.3;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Agency's observations about whether the
    information referred to in paragraph (b) discloses any systemic or
    emerging issues in respect of any matter or thing that concerns
    transportation to which the legislative authority of Parliament
    extends and that relates to the mobility of persons with
    disabilities; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other information prescribed by regulations
    made under subsection (2.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2.01)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (2)(d), the Governor
  in Council may make regulations prescribing information about any
  matter or thing that concerns transportation to which the legislative
  authority of Parliament extends and that relates to the mobility of
  persons with disabilities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Railway transportation

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The report shall include the number and nature of
  the applications, complaints and submissions for arbitration made
  under Parts III and IV, the manner they were dealt with and the
  systemic trends observed. The report shall also include the number of
  disputes that were mediated by the Agency and the number that were
  resolved through mediation by the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential information

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall ensure that the report does not
  include any confidential information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling of report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall have a copy of each report made
  under this section laid before each House of Parliament on any of the
  first thirty days on which that House is sitting after the Minister
  receives it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 42
- 2013, c. 31, s. 2
- 2018, c. 10, s. 6

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154739-1191850.html"
  role="button"}

### [Governor in Council]{.HTitleText2} {#h-54433 .Subheading}

#### [Directions to Agency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54434 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Policy directions

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, at the request of the Agency or of the
  Governor in Council's own motion, issue policy directions to the
  Agency concerning any matter that comes within the jurisdiction of the
  Agency and every such direction shall be carried out by the Agency
  under the Act of Parliament that establishes the powers, duties and
  functions of the Agency in relation to the subject-matter of the
  direction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A direction issued under subsection (1) shall not
  affect a matter that is before the Agency on the date of the direction
  and that relates to a particular person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay of binding effect

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** A direction issued under
section 43 is not binding on the Agency until the expiration of the
thirtieth sitting day of Parliament after the direction has been laid
before both Houses of Parliament by or on behalf of the Minister, unless
the direction has been previously laid before both Houses of Parliament
in proposed form by or on behalf of the Minister and thirty sitting days
of Parliament have expired after the proposed direction was laid.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to committee

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** Where a direction
referred to in section 43 is issued or a proposed direction referred to
in section 44 is laid before a House of Parliament, it shall be referred
without delay by that House to the committee of that House that it
considers appropriate to deal with the subject-matter of the direction
or proposed direction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation required

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** Before a direction
referred to in section 43 is issued or a proposed direction referred to
in section 44 is laid before a House of Parliament, the Minister shall
consult with the Agency with respect to the nature and subject-matter of
the direction or proposed direction.

#### [Extraordinary Disruptions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54446 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council may prevent
disruptions

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the Governor in Council is of the opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an extraordinary disruption to the effective
    continued operation of the national transportation system exists or
    is imminent, other than a labour disruption,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} failure to act under this section would be contrary
    to the interests of users and operators of the national
    transportation system, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there are no other provisions in this Act or in any
    other Act of Parliament that are sufficient and appropriate to
    remedy the situation and counter the actual or anticipated damage
    caused by the disruption,

  the Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister and
  the minister responsible for the Bureau of Competition Policy, by
  order, take any steps, or direct the Agency to take any steps, that
  the Governor in Council considers essential to stabilize the national
  transportation system, including the imposition of capacity and
  pricing restraints.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may consult affected persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before recommending that an order be made under this
  section, the Minister may consult with any person who the Minister
  considers may be affected by the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order is temporary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made under this section shall have effect
  for no more than ninety days after the order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be tabled in Parliament

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall cause any order made under this
  section to be laid before both Houses of Parliament within seven
  sitting days after the order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to Parliamentary Committee

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every order laid before Parliament under
  subsection (4) shall be referred for review to the Standing committee
  designated by Parliament for the purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution of Parliament revoking order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a resolution directing that an order made under
  this section be revoked is adopted by both Houses of Parliament before
  the expiration of thirty sitting days of Parliament after the order is
  laid before both Houses of Parliament, the order shall cease to have
  effect on the day that the resolution is adopted or, if the adopted
  resolution specifies a day on which the order shall cease to have
  effect, on that specified day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34)

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection 4(2), this section and
  anything done under the authority of this section prevail over the
  [Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes an order made under this
  section is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an individual, to a fine not
    exceeding \$5,000, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, to a fine not
    exceeding \$100,000,

  for each day the person contravenes the order.

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1414028 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance data of air carriers

**[[47.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations requiring air carriers to publish
information respecting their performance on their Internet site.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 437](#1410585-1414027){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410585-1414027.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Minister]{.HTitleText2} {#h-54471 .Subheading}

#### [Authorization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1414029 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers and duties

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, in
writing, authorize any person designated by the Minister to exercise any
of the powers and perform any of the duties of the Minister under this
Act, either generally or otherwise provided in the instrument of
authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 48
- [2018, c. 10, s. 7](#1410587-1414034){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-48-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 48"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fees and Charges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1414036 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[48.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may make
regulations respecting fees and charges to be paid to the Minister in
relation to the administration and enforcement of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 438](#1410587-1414040){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410587-1414040.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Inquiries]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54473 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may request inquiry

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may direct the Agency to inquire into any matter or thing
  concerning transportation to which the legislative authority of
  Parliament extends and report the findings on the inquiry to the
  Minister as and when the Minister may require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, sections 38 and 39 apply in
  respect of an inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary of findings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall make public a summary of its
  findings that does not include any confidential information.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 49
- 2018, c. 10, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-49-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 49"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Transportation Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-54483 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations re information

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations requiring any persons
  referred to in subsection (1.1) who are subject to the legislative
  authority of Parliament to provide information, other than personal
  information as defined in section 3 of the [Privacy
  Act](/eng/acts/P-21), to the Minister, when and in the form and manner
  that the regulations may specify, for the purposes of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} national transportation policy development;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reporting under section 52;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} operational planning;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any safety, security or subsidy program;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any infrastructure requirement;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} monitoring the grain transportation and handling
    system; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the administration of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Efficiency of system

  [(1.001)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  requiring any persons referred to in subsection (1.1) who are subject
  to the legislative authority of Parliament and any users, other than
  passengers, of the national transportation system to provide
  information, other than [[personal information]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  as defined in section 3 of the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21), to the
  Minister, a person designated under section 6.11, any persons referred
  to in subsection (1.1) who are subject to the legislative authority of
  Parliament or any users, other than passengers, of the national
  transportation system, when and in the form and manner that the
  regulations may specify, for the purposes of ensuring the proper
  functioning of the national transportation system or increasing its
  efficiency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class 1 rail carrier

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  requiring any class 1 rail carrier or class of those carriers to
  provide information, other than [[personal
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in section 3 of the
  [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21), to the Minister or Agency, when and in
  the form and manner that the regulations may specify, for the purposes
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determining the long-haul interswitching rate
    referred to in paragraph 134(1)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicating service and performance indicators to
    the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons referred to

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The persons for the purposes of subsection (1) are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carriers;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} owners or operators of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} transportation undertakings,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} transportation works, infrastructure, facilities
      or assets, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} grain handling undertakings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} providers of services in relation to
    transportation, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian Air Transport Security Authority,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} NAV CANADA, a corporation incorporated on May
      26, 1995 under Part II of the [Canada Corporations
      Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of the Revised Statutes of
      Canada, 1970, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} Pilotage Authorities named in the schedule to
      the [Pilotage Act](/eng/acts/P-14);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} intermediaries involved in transportation movements
    who are specified in the regulations; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other person or class of persons specified in
    the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be provided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Information that is required to be provided under
  this section may include the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information respecting traffic and operations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fitness and ownership information; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} information respecting the performance of air
    carriers and providers of services in relation to air transportation
    with regard to passenger experience and the quality of service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No regulation made under subsection (1) or (1.001)
  shall require or have the effect of requiring any person to provide
  the Minister, a person designated under section 6.11, any persons
  referred to in subsection (1.1) who are subject to the legislative
  authority of Parliament or any users, other than passengers, of the
  national transportation system with a contract referred to in
  subsection 68(1) or a contract entered into under subsection 126(1) or
  under section 53 of the [Canada Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of a
  contract entered into under subsection 126(1) to the extent that the
  information is required for the purpose of monitoring the grain
  transportation and handling system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report on the monitoring of the grain
  transportation and handling system

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must prepare, within six months after
  the end of each crop year, a report on the monitoring of the grain
  transportation and handling system and cause the report to be tabled
  in each House of Parliament on any of the first fifteen days on which
  that House is sitting after the Minister prepares it, if the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes a regulation under paragraph (1)(e.1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} uses or communicates the information provided under
    the regulation for the purpose of monitoring the grain
    transportation and handling system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may exempt a carrier or transportation
  undertaking from the application of all or any part of a regulation
  made under this section if the Minister is satisfied that it is not
  practicable for the carrier or transportation undertaking to provide
  the information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may consult with the Agency or
  Statistics Canada before making any regulation under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 50
- 1998, c. 10, s. 163
- 1999, c. 31, s. 36(E)
- 2000, c. 16, s. 1
- 2007, c. 19, s. 8
- 2013, c. 31, s. 3(F)
- 2018, c. 10, s. 9
- [2023, c. 26, s. 439](#1410589-1414045){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410589-1414045.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 50"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Externally produced documents

- **[[50.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection
  50(1) or (1.01) may incorporate by reference any document that is
  produced by a person or body other than the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Type of incorporation by reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document may be incorporated by reference either as
  it exists on a particular date or as it is amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility of incorporated document

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must ensure that a document that is
  incorporated by reference is accessible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No finding of guilt or administrative
  sanction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person is not liable to be found guilty of an
  offence or subjected to an administrative sanction for any
  contravention in respect of which a document that is incorporated by
  reference is relevant unless, at the time of the alleged
  contravention, the document was accessible as required by
  subsection (3) or it was otherwise accessible to that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No registration or publication

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a document that is
  incorporated by reference is not required to be transmitted for
  registration or published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) by reason only that it is
  incorporated by reference.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 4
- 2018, c. 10, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50.01-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 50.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Existing power not limited

**[[50.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.02 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, an express power in this Act to incorporate by reference does
not limit the power that otherwise exists to incorporate by reference in
a regulation made under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information already provided

**[[50.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 50(1) or (1.01), if any information referred to in that
subsection has already been provided to a department or agency of the
Government of Canada, the Minister may request that department or agency
to provide the information to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 9
- 2018, c. 10, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50.1-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 50.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of information ---
Minister or designated person

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as otherwise specifically provided in this Act or any other Act of
  Parliament, information required to be provided under this Act to the
  Minister or a person designated under section 6.11 is, when it is
  received by the Minister or the designated person, confidential and
  must not knowingly be disclosed or made available by any person
  without the authorization of the person who provided the information,
  except for the purposes of a prosecution of a contravention of section
  173.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply so as to prohibit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information to the Agency, the
    Administrator of the Fund appointed under section 153.7, Statistics
    Canada or a minister of the Crown in right of Canada, the agent of
    that minister or an officer or employee of, or adviser to, Her
    Majesty in right of Canada for the purposes of the administration of
    this Act or any other Act of Parliament or for the purposes of the
    development of policies;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information that is provided
    under regulations made under subsection 50(1.001) to persons
    referred to in subsection 50(1.1) who are subject to the legislative
    authority of Parliament or any users, other than passengers, of the
    national transportation system;

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information prescribed in
    the regulations to persons prescribed in the regulations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information to persons
    referred to in paragraph 50(1.1)(c) that is necessary for them to
    carry out their duties and functions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information, including to the
    public, in an aggregated form that prevents information obtained
    from an identifiable person from being related to that person;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information by the Minister
    for the purpose of monitoring the grain transportation and handling
    system; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information that is available
    to, or ascertainable by, the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, with the approval of the Governor
  in Council, make regulations respecting the terms and conditions for
  the communication of information referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  prescribing information and persons for the purposes of paragraph
  (2)(a.2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe and secure procedures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister and any person designated under section
  6.11 shall ensure that the procedures and physical measures taken to
  ensure the confidentiality of information provided to them under this
  Act, including the keeping of electronic data, are safe and secure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for other persons to
  maintain confidentiality

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any person who receives from the Minister or a person
  designated under section 6.11 information that is confidential under
  this Act shall not knowingly disclose that information and shall take
  the measures necessary to maintain its confidentiality.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 51
- 2000, c. 16, s. 2
- 2007, c. 19, s. 10
- 2018, c. 10, s. 12
- [2023, c. 26, s. 440](#1410594-1414056){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::
