[Previous
Version](section-86-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 86"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advertising regulations

- **[[86.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall make regulations
  respecting advertising in all media, including on the Internet, of
  prices for air services within, or originating in, Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the generality of subsection (1),
  regulations shall be made under that subsection requiring a carrier
  who advertises a price for an air service to include in the price all
  costs to the carrier of providing the service and to indicate in the
  advertisement all fees, charges and taxes collected by the carrier on
  behalf of another person in respect of the service, so as to enable a
  purchaser of the service to readily determine the total amount to be
  paid for the service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations may prescribe

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the generality of subsection (1),
  the regulations may prescribe what are costs, fees, charges and taxes
  for the purposes of subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- carrier's obligations
towards passengers

- **[[86.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, after consulting
  with the Minister, make regulations in relation to flights to, from
  and within Canada, including connecting flights,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the carrier's obligation to make terms
    and conditions of carriage and information regarding any recourse
    available against the carrier, as specified in the regulations,
    readily available to passengers in language that is simple, clear
    and concise;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the carrier's obligations in the case of
    flight delay, flight cancellation or denial of boarding, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the minimum standards of treatment of passengers
      that the carrier is required to meet and the minimum compensation
      the carrier is required to pay for inconvenience when the delay,
      cancellation or denial of boarding is within the carrier's
      control,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the minimum standards of treatment of passengers
      that the carrier is required to meet when the delay, cancellation
      or denial of boarding is within the carrier's control, but is
      required for safety purposes, including in situations of
      mechanical malfunctions,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the carrier's obligation to ensure that
      passengers complete their itinerary when the delay, cancellation
      or denial of boarding is due to situations outside the carrier's
      control, such as natural phenomena and security events, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the carrier's obligation to provide timely
      information and assistance to passengers;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the minimum compensation for delayed,
    lost or damaged baggage that the carrier is required to pay;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the carrier's obligation to facilitate
    the assignment of seats to children under the age of 14 years in
    close proximity to a parent, guardian or tutor at no additional cost
    and to make the carrier's terms and conditions and practices in this
    respect readily available to passengers;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} requiring the carrier to establish terms and
    conditions of carriage with regard to the transportation of musical
    instruments;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the carrier's obligations in the case of
    tarmac delays over three hours, including the obligation to provide
    timely information and assistance to passengers, as well as the
    minimum standards of treatment of passengers that the carrier is
    required to meet; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting any of the carrier's other obligations
    that the Minister may issue directions on under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may issue directions to the Agency to
  make a regulation under paragraph (1)(g) respecting any of the
  carrier's other obligations towards passengers. The Agency shall
  comply with these directions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not receive compensation from a
  carrier under regulations made under subsection (1) if that person has
  already received compensation for the same event under a different
  passenger rights regime than the one provided for under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations deemed to be in tariffs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The carrier's obligations established by a regulation
  made under subsection (1) are deemed to form part of the terms and
  conditions set out in the carrier's tariffs in so far as the carrier's
  tariffs do not provide more advantageous terms and conditions of
  carriage than those obligations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 19
- [2023, c. 26, s. 465](#1410665-1414115){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410665-1414115.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86.11-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 86.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations and orders

**[[86.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.2 .sectionLabel}** A regulation or order
made under this Part may be conditional or unconditional or qualified or
unqualified and may be general or restricted to a specific area, person
or thing or group or class of persons or things.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 27
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Railway Transportation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-55143 .Part}

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Interpretation and Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-55144 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[crude oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[crude oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means virgin or naturally
    occurring unrefined petroleum, or diluted bitumen or any other
    unrefined hydrocarbon mixture that is similar in composition to
    virgin or naturally occurring unrefined petroleum. It includes crude
    oil with the UN numbers 1267 and 3494 that are set out in Column 1
    of the Dangerous Goods List in Chapter 3.2 of the Recommendations on
    the Transport of Dangerous Goods -- Model Regulations, Eighteenth
    revised edition, 2013, published by the United Nations; ([pétrole
    brut]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an interest in land and, in
    relation to land in the Province of Quebec, includes the interest of
    a lessee; ([terres]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[metropolitan area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[metropolitan area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any area that is
    classified by Statistics Canada in its most recent census of Canada
    as a census metropolitan area; ([région
    métropolitaine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[operate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes, with respect to a railway,
    any act necessary for the maintenance of the railway or the
    operation of a train; ([exploitation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[point of destination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[point of destination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to
    traffic on a railway line that is subject to a transfer described in
    subsection 128(4) or section 131, the point where the traffic is
    transferred from the line of a railway company to a line to which
    this Part does not apply; ([point de destination]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[point of origin]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[point of origin]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to
    traffic on a railway line that is subject to a transfer described in
    subsection 128(4) or section 131, the point where the traffic is
    transferred to the line of a railway company from a line to which
    this Part does not apply; ([point d'origine]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[public passenger service provider]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[public passenger service provider]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means VIA
    Rail Canada Inc., a passenger rail service provider designated by
    the Minister or an urban transit authority; ([société de transport
    publique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Quebec--Windsor corridor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Quebec--Windsor corridor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the area of
    Canada that is bounded

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the east by longitude 70.50° W,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the north by a straight line connecting a
      first point located at latitude 47.45° N and longitude 70.50° W to
      a second point located at latitude 43.70° N and longitude 83.25°
      W,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to the west by longitude 83.25° W, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to the south by the Canada-United States
      border; ([axe Québec-Windsor]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[railway]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[railway]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a railway within the
    legislative authority of Parliament and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} branches, extensions, sidings, railway bridges,
      tunnels, stations, depots, wharfs, rolling stock, equipment,
      stores, or other things connected with the railway, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communications or signalling systems and related
      facilities and equipment used for railway purposes; ([chemin de
      fer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who holds a
    certificate of fitness under section 92, a partnership of such
    persons or a person who is mentioned in subsection
    90(2); ([compagnie de chemin de fer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[road]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[road]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any way or course, whether public
    or not, available for vehicular or pedestrian
    use; ([route]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Special Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Special Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an Act under which a
    railway company has authority to construct or operate a railway, or
    that is enacted with special reference to a railway, and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} letters patent constituting a company's authority
      to construct or operate a railway, granted before April 1, 1969,
      under any Act, and the Act under which the letters patent were
      granted or confirmed, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} letters patent incorporating a company under
      section 11 of the Railway Act on or after April 1, 1969, whose
      objects are the construction or operation of a railway in
      Canada; ([loi spéciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[tariff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[tariff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a schedule of rates, charges,
    terms and conditions applicable to the movement of traffic and
    incidental services; ([tarif]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[traffic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[traffic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the traffic of goods,
    including equipment required for their
    movement; ([transport]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou
    [trafic]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[urban transit authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[urban transit authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an entity
    owned or controlled by the federal government or a provincial,
    municipal or district government that provides commuter
    services; ([administration de transport de
    banlieue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Vancouver--Kamloops corridor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Vancouver--Kamloops corridor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the area
    of Canada that is bounded

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the east by longitude 121.21° W,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the north by latitude 50.83° N,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to the west by longitude 128.45° W, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to the south by the Canada-United States
      border. ([axe Vancouver-Kamloops]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 87
- 2007, c. 19, s. 28
- 2015, c. 31, s. 4
- 2018, c. 10, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-87-20160618.html "Link to previous version of section 87"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies to all persons, railway companies and railways within the
  legislative authority of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special cases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the effect of subsection (1), this
  Part applies to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a company operating a railway from the United
    States into Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a railway, or a portion of a railway, whether or
    not constructed under the authority of an Act of Parliament, that is
    owned, controlled, leased or operated by a person who operates a
    railway within the legislative authority of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Works for the general advantage of
  Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A railway or a portion of a railway mentioned in
  paragraph (2)(b) is declared to be a work for the general advantage of
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of declaration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A declaration by subsection (3) or by any other Act
  of Parliament that a railway is a work for the general advantage of
  Canada, or for the advantage of two or more provinces, ceases to have
  effect with respect to a line of the railway, or a portion of it,
  whose operation is discontinued under Division V or is the subject of
  a transfer under that Division to a person other than a company
  mentioned in paragraph (2)(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special Act railways declared to be works
for general advantage of Canada

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** If the construction or
operation of a railway is authorized by a Special Act passed by the
legislature of a province and the railway is declared by an Act of
Parliament to be a work for the general advantage of Canada, this Part
applies to the railway to the exclusion of any general railway Act of
the province and any provisions of the Special Act that are inconsistent
with this Part.

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Construction and Operation of Railways]{.HTitleText2} {#h-55190 .Subheading}

#### [Certificate of Fitness]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55191 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate required

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} construct a railway without being the holder of a
    certificate of fitness that is issued under paragraph 92(1)(a); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operate a railway without being the holder of a
    certificate of fitness that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued under paragraph 92(1)(a) for the portion
      of the operation that relates to a passenger rail service, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} issued under paragraph 92(1)(b) for the portion
      of the operation that does not relate to a passenger rail service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- purchaser of railway

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a purchaser of a
  railway, or a portion of a railway, for a period of sixty days after
  the purchase if the railway or portion is purchased

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} under a trust deed or mortgage;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the instance of the holders of a mortgage, bond
    or debenture for the payment of which a charge has been created on
    the railway or portion; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} under any other lawful proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 90
- 2015, c. 31, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-90-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for certificate of fitness

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person may apply for a certificate of fitness for a railway, including
  a person who owns or leases the railway or controls, either directly
  or indirectly, a person who owns or leases the railway.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for certain types of
  operations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person proposes to operate in Canada primarily
  on the railway of another railway company, the application must
  indicate the termini and route of every line of railway proposed to be
  operated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of certificate of fitness

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency shall issue a certificate of fitness

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the proposed operation of a railway that
    relates to a passenger rail service or for the proposed construction
    of a railway, if the Agency is satisfied that there will be adequate
    liability insurance coverage for the proposed operation or
    construction, as determined in accordance with the regulations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the proposed operation of a railway that does
    not relate to a passenger rail service, if the Agency is satisfied
    that there will be, for the proposed operation, the applicable
    minimum liability insurance coverage, which includes any
    self-insurance, as set out in Schedule IV.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Covered risks

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The liability insurance referred to in paragraph
  (1)(b) must cover the following risks that may arise out of the
  proposed operation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} third-party bodily injury or death, including
    injury or death to passengers;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} third-party property damage, excluding damage to
    goods carried on a shipper's behalf;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} risks that are associated with a leak, pollution or
    contamination; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a railway accident as defined in
    section 152.5, the other losses, damages, costs and expenses
    described in subsection 153(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Self-insurance

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(b), the amount of
  self-insurance must not exceed the maximum amount of self-insurance
  that the person who will be responsible for the proposed operation can
  sustain based on that person's financial capability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termini and route

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of fitness issued pursuant to an
  application mentioned in subsection 91(2) must specify the termini and
  route of every railway line to be operated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations made by Agency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the proposed operation of a railway
    that relates to a passenger rail service or of a proposed
    construction of a railway, for determining the adequacy of liability
    insurance coverage, including any self-insurance; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the proposed operation of a railway
    that does not relate to a passenger rail service, respecting the
    provision of information that the Agency requires to determine
    whether there will be, for the proposed operation, the applicable
    minimum liability insurance coverage, which includes any
    self-insurance, as set out in Schedule IV.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations made by Governor in Council

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by regulation, amend
  Schedule IV by deleting or adding a class of railway operations and an
  amount of minimum liability insurance coverage or by amending such a
  class --- including by adding or deleting factors that define its
  scope --- or such an amount.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 92
- 2015, c. 31, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-92-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 92"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of certificate

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may, on application, vary a certificate of fitness

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to change the termini or route of a line specified
    in the certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to add a line to the certificate; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to reflect a change in railway operations or
    circumstances relating to those operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation when running rights granted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may vary a certificate of fitness when it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes an order under paragraph 116(4)(e) that
    requires a railway company to grant a right to the holder of the
    certificate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} grants a right under section 138 to the holder of
    the certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 93
- 2000, c. 16, s. 3
:::

#### [Liability Insurance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55246 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing requirement

- **[[93.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a certificate of
  fitness shall maintain at all times,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the certificate was issued under paragraph
    92(1)(a), adequate liability insurance coverage, as determined in
    accordance with the regulations, for the operation or construction
    of the railway for which the certificate was issued; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the certificate was issued under paragraph
    92(1)(b), the applicable minimum liability insurance coverage, which
    includes any self-insurance, as set out in Schedule IV, for the
    operation of the railway for which the certificate was issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Covered risks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The liability insurance referred to in paragraph
  (1)(b) must cover the risks described in paragraphs 92(1.1)(a) to (d)
  that may arise out of the operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Self-insurance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(b), the amount of
  self-insurance must not exceed the maximum amount of self-insurance
  that the holder of the certificate of fitness can sustain based on its
  financial capability.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 31, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of insurance changes

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** The holder of a
certificate of fitness shall notify the Agency in writing without delay

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the liability insurance coverage is cancelled or
  altered; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there are any changes to the construction or
  operation that may affect the liability insurance coverage.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 94
- 2015, c. 31, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry

**[[94.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.1 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may make
an inquiry to determine whether a holder of a certificate of fitness
complies with section 93.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 31, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension or cancellation of certificate
of fitness

**[[94.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.2 .sectionLabel}** The Agency shall
suspend or cancel a certificate of fitness if it determines that the
holder of the certificate has failed to comply with section 93.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 31, s. 7
:::

#### [General Powers of Railway Companies]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55272 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the provisions of this
  Part and any other Act of Parliament, a railway company may exercise
  the following powers for the purpose of constructing or operating its
  railway:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make or construct tunnels, embankments, aqueducts,
    bridges, roads, conduits, drains, piers, arches, cuttings and fences
    across or along a railway, watercourse, canal or road that adjoins
    or intersects the railway;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} divert or alter the course of a watercourse or
    road, or raise or lower it, in order to carry it more conveniently
    across or along the railway;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make drains or conduits into, through or under land
    adjoining the railway for the purpose of conveying water from or to
    the railway;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} divert or alter the position of a water pipe, gas
    pipe, sewer or drain, or telegraph, telephone or electric line, wire
    or pole across or along the railway; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} do anything else necessary for the construction or
    operation of the railway.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimal damage

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall do as little damage as
  possible in the exercise of the powers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restoration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the railway company diverts or alters anything
  mentioned in paragraph (1)(b) or (d), the company shall restore it as
  nearly as possible to its former condition, or shall put it in a
  condition that does not substantially impair its usefulness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall pay compensation to a
  person who sustains actual loss or damage from the exercise of the
  powers and the compensation must equal the amount of the loss or
  damage that the company would be liable to pay the person if the
  powers had not been conferred by statute.

#### [Noise and Vibration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55287 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation

**[[95.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95.1 .sectionLabel}** When constructing or
operating a railway, a railway company shall cause only such noise and
vibration as is reasonable, taking into account

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} its obligations under sections 113 and 114, if
  applicable;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} its operational requirements; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the area where the construction or operation takes
  place.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines

- **[[95.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall issue, and publish
  in any manner that it considers appropriate, guidelines with respect
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the elements that the Agency will use to determine
    whether a railway company is complying with section 95.1; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the collaborative resolution of noise and vibration
    complaints relating to the construction or operation of railways.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency must consult with interested parties,
  including municipal governments, before issuing any guidelines.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not statutory instruments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The guidelines are not statutory instruments within
  the meaning of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints and investigations

- **[[95.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of a complaint made by any person that a railway company is
  not complying with section 95.1, the Agency may order the railway
  company to undertake any changes in its railway construction or
  operation that the Agency considers reasonable to ensure compliance
  with that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency has published guidelines under
  paragraph 95.2(1)(b), it must first satisfy itself that the
  collaborative measures set out in the guidelines have been exhausted
  in respect of the noise or vibration complained of before it conducts
  any investigation or hearing in respect of the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public passenger service providers

**[[95.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95.4 .sectionLabel}** Sections 95.1 to 95.3
apply, with any modifications that are necessary, to public passenger
service providers.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 29
:::

#### [Land Transfer]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55317 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Land taken pursuant to section 134 of
Railway Act

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a railway company took possession of, used or occupied land under
  section 134 of the Railway Act before the coming into force of section
  185, the railway company may not alienate the land except to transfer
  it to a railway company for the purpose of continuing railway
  operations or to the Crown.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alienation to other persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The land may be transferred to any other person for
  the purpose of continuing railway operations if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of land in Quebec, the transfer
    involves only a lease or a dismemberment of the right of ownership;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of land in any other province, the
    transfer does not involve a transfer of the fee simple in the land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing application to subsequent
  transfers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If land is transferred under subsection (1) or (2) to
  any person other than the Crown in right of Canada, subsequent
  transfers of the land are subject to the same limitations as those
  contained in this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Existing rights

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A transfer of land under this section does not affect
  any right or interest of a person, other than a railway company, that
  existed in the land immediately before the coming into force of
  section 185.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Land obtained for railway purposes

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 96, a railway
  company that obtained land from the Crown or any other person to
  assist in the construction or operation of its railway may acquire or
  transfer the land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of authority

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the railway company transfers the land to another
  company that has entered into a contract for the construction or
  operation, in whole or in part, of the railway for which the land was
  obtained, the other company has the same authority under this section
  as the railway company had in respect of the land.

#### [Railway Lines]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55334 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No construction without Agency approval

- **[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company shall not construct a railway line without the
  approval of the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grant of approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, on application by the railway
  company, grant the approval if it considers that the location of the
  railway line is reasonable, taking into consideration requirements for
  railway operations and services and the interests of the localities
  that will be affected by the line.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No approval is needed for the construction of a
  railway line

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within the right of way of an existing railway
    line; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 100 m of the centre line of an existing
    railway line for a distance of no more than 3 km.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing agreements

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  agreement, or an amendment to an agreement, relating to the
  construction of a railway line across another railway line may be
  filed with the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When the agreement or amendment is filed, it becomes
  an order of the Agency authorizing the parties to construct the
  railway line as provided in the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person is unsuccessful in negotiating an
  agreement or amendment mentioned in subsection (1), the Agency may, on
  application, authorize the construction of the railway line or any
  related work.

#### [Road and Utility Crossings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55351 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** In this section and
section 101,

[[road crossing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[road crossing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the part of a road that
    passes across, over or under a railway line, and includes a
    structure supporting or protecting that part of the road or
    facilitating the crossing; ([franchissement
    routier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[utility crossing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[utility crossing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the part of a utility
    line that passes over or under a railway line, and includes a
    structure supporting or protecting that part of the utility line or
    facilitating the crossing; ([franchissement par
    desserte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[utility line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[utility line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a wire, cable, pipeline
    or other like means of enabling the transmission of goods or energy
    or the provision of services. ([desserte]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing agreements

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  agreement, or an amendment to an agreement, relating to the
  construction, maintenance or apportionment of the costs of a road
  crossing or a utility crossing may be filed with the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When the agreement or amendment is filed, it becomes
  an order of the Agency authorizing the parties to construct or
  maintain the crossing, or apportioning the costs, as provided in the
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No agreement on construction or
  maintenance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person is unsuccessful in negotiating an
  agreement or amendment mentioned in subsection (1), the Agency may, on
  application, authorize the construction of a suitable road crossing,
  utility crossing or related work, or specifying who shall maintain the
  crossing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No agreement on apportionment of costs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 16 of the [Railway Safety
  Act](/eng/acts/R-4.2) applies if a person is unsuccessful in
  negotiating an agreement relating to the apportionment of the costs of
  constructing or maintaining the road crossing or utility crossing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of section

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in any circumstances
  where section 102 or 103 applies.

#### [Private Crossings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55368 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Divided land

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** If an owner's land is
divided as a result of the construction of a railway line, the railway
company shall, at the owner's request, construct a suitable crossing for
the owner's enjoyment of the land.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other crossings may be ordered

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  railway company and an owner of land adjoining the company's railway
  do not agree on the construction of a crossing across the railway, the
  Agency, on the application of the owner, may order the company to
  construct a suitable crossing if the Agency considers it necessary for
  the owner's enjoyment of the land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may include in its order terms and
  conditions governing the construction and maintenance of the crossing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of construction and maintenance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The owner of the land shall pay the costs of
  constructing and maintaining the crossing.

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Financial Transactions of Railway Companies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-55378 .Subheading}

#### [Security]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55379 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit of mortgage, hypothec or security
agreement

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following may, in accordance with the regulations, be deposited by any
  person in the office of the Registrar General of Canada or in any
  other place that the Governor in Council may, by order, specify:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a mortgage or hypothec issued by a railway company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a security agreement entered into by a railway
    company;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an assignment or other document affecting a
    document referred to in paragraph (a) or (b); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a copy of any document referred to in any of
    paragraphs (a) to (c) or a summary of any such document made in
    accordance with regulations made under section 105.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of deposit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Once the deposit is made, the mortgage or hypothec,
  security agreement, assignment or other document need not be
  deposited, registered or filed under any other law or statute
  respecting real or personal property, and the deposited document is
  valid against all persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 104
- 1999, c. 31, s. 38(E)
- 2007, c. 19, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 104"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Documents Dealing with Rolling Stock]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55391 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit of documents

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  document, or a copy or summary of a document, evidencing any of the
  following transactions may, in accordance with the regulations, be
  deposited by any person in the office of the Registrar General of
  Canada or in any other place that the Governor in Council may, by
  order, specify:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a lease, sale, conditional sale, instalment sale,
    mortgage, hypothec, bailment, leasing, deposit or security agreement
    relating to rolling stock or any accessories or appurtenances
    relating to rolling stock; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amendment, assignment or discharge of a document
    mentioned in paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2007, c. 19, s. 31\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of deposit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Once the deposit is made, the document need not be
  deposited, registered or filed under any other law or statute
  respecting real or personal property, and the document is valid
  against all persons.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2007, c. 19, s. 31\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 105
- 2007, c. 19, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55403 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[105.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the depositing of copies of documents under sections
  104 and 105;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the form and content of summaries to be deposited
  under those sections; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the effects, in Canada, of the deposit, registration
  or filing in other countries of documents evidencing any transaction
  referred to in paragraph 105(1)(a) or of documents evidencing
  amendments, assignments or discharges of those documents.

:::

### [DIVISION IV]{.HLabel2}[Rates, Tariffs and Services]{.HTitleText2} {#h-55416 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55417 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[competitive line rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[competitive line rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2018, c.
    10, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

[[confidential contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[confidential contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a contract
    entered into under subsection 126(1); ([contrat
    confidentiel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[connecting carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[connecting carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a railway company,
    other than a local carrier, that moves traffic to or from an
    interchange over a portion of a continuous route; ([transporteur de
    liaison]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[interchange]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[interchange]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a place where the line of
    one railway company connects with the line of another railway
    company and where loaded or empty cars may be stored until delivered
    or received by the other railway company; ([lieu de
    correspondance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[interswitch]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[interswitch]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to transfer traffic from
    the lines of one railway company to the lines of another railway
    company; ([interconnexion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[interswitching rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[interswitching rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a rate determined
    by the Agency in accordance with section 127.1; ([Version anglaise
    seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[local carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[local carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a class 1 rail carrier
    that moves traffic to or from an interchange on a continuous route
    from the point of origin or to the point of destination that is
    served exclusively by the class 1 rail carrier; ([transporteur
    local]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[long-haul interswitching rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[long-haul interswitching rate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a rate
    determined by the Agency in accordance with paragraph
    134(1)(a); ([Version anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[service obligations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[service obligations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means obligations under
    section 113 or 114. ([Version anglaise
    seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 111
- 2018, c. 10, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Rates and Conditions of Service]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55431 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commercially fair and reasonable

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** A rate or condition of
service established by the Agency under this Division must be
commercially fair and reasonable to all parties.

#### [Level of Services]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55434 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accommodation for traffic

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company shall, according to its powers, in respect of a
  railway owned or operated by it,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} furnish, at the point of origin, at the point of
    junction of the railway with another railway, and at all points of
    stopping established for that purpose, adequate and suitable
    accommodation for the receiving and loading of all traffic offered
    for carriage on the railway;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} furnish adequate and suitable accommodation for the
    carriage, unloading and delivering of the traffic;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} without delay, and with due care and diligence,
    receive, carry and deliver the traffic;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} furnish and use all proper appliances,
    accommodation and means necessary for receiving, loading, carrying,
    unloading and delivering the traffic; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} furnish any other service incidental to
    transportation that is customary or usual in connection with the
    business of a railway company.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Carriage on payment of rates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Traffic must be taken, carried to and from, and
  delivered at the points referred to in paragraph (1)(a) on the payment
  of the lawfully payable rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Carriage on payment of levy

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If a railway company is to carry traffic in respect
  of which there is a levy under section 155.3 or 155.5, the traffic
  must be carried from a point referred to in paragraph (1)(a) by the
  railway company on the payment to the company of the levy, by the
  shipper, if the company is the first railway company to carry, at a
  rate other than an interswitching rate, the traffic after its loading.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation for provision of rolling
  stock

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a shipper provides rolling stock for the
  carriage by the railway company of the shipper's traffic, the company
  shall, at the request of the shipper, establish specific reasonable
  compensation to the shipper in a tariff for the provision of the
  rolling stock.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential contract between company
  and shipper

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A shipper and a railway company may, by means of a
  confidential contract or other written agreement, agree on the manner
  in which the obligations under this section are to be fulfilled by the
  company.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 113
- 2015, c. 31, s. 8
- 2018, c. 10, s. 59(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-113-20160618.html "Link to previous version of section 113"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facilities for traffic

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company shall, according to its powers, afford to all persons
  and other companies all adequate and suitable accommodation for
  receiving, carrying and delivering traffic on and from its railway,
  for the transfer of traffic between its railway and other railways and
  for the return of rolling stock.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Through traffic

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), adequate and
  suitable accommodation includes reasonable facilities for the
  receiving, carriage and delivery by the company

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the request of any other company, of through
    traffic and, in the case of goods shipped by carload, of the car
    with the goods shipped in it, to and from the railway of the other
    company, at a through rate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the request of any person interested in through
    traffic, of such traffic at through rates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Connecting railway to reasonable
  facilities

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every railway company that has or operates a railway
  forming part of a continuous line of railway with or that intersects
  any other railway, or that has any terminus, station or wharf near to
  any terminus, station or wharf of another railway, shall afford all
  reasonable facilities for delivering to that other railway, or for
  receiving from or carrying by its railway, all the traffic arriving by
  that other railway without any unreasonable delay, so that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no obstruction is offered to the public desirous of
    using those railways as a continuous line of communication; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all reasonable accommodation, by means of the
    railways of those companies, is at all times afforded to the public
    for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Similar facilities for truckers

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a railway company provides facilities for the
  transportation by rail of motor vehicles or trailers operated by any
  company under its control for the conveyance of goods for hire or
  reward,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the railway company shall offer to all companies
    operating motor vehicles or trailers for the conveyance of goods for
    hire or reward similar facilities at the same rates and on the same
    terms and conditions as those applicable to the motor vehicles or
    trailers operated by the company under its control; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Agency may disallow any rate or tariff that is
    not in compliance with this subsection and direct the company to
    substitute a rate or tariff that complies with this subsection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adequate and suitable accommodation

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 113(1) or 114(1), adequate and suitable accommodation
includes reasonable facilities

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the junction of private sidings or private spurs
  with a railway owned or operated by a company referred to in that
  subsection; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for receiving, carrying and delivering traffic on and
  from private sidings or private spurs and placing cars and moving them
  on and from those private sidings or private spurs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint and investigation concerning
company's obligations

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of a complaint made by any person that a railway company is
  not fulfilling any of its service obligations, the Agency shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct, as expeditiously as possible, an
    investigation of the complaint that, in its opinion, is warranted;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 90 days after receipt of the complaint,
    determine whether the company is fulfilling that obligation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of an investigation conducted under
  subsection (1), the Agency shall allow a company at least 20 days to
  file an answer and at least 10 days for a complainant to file a reply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency's own motion

  [(1.11)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, with the authorization of the
  Minister and subject to any terms and conditions that the Minister
  considers appropriate, of its own motion, conduct an investigation to
  determine whether a railway company is fulfilling its service
  obligations. The Agency shall conduct the investigation as
  expeditiously as possible and make its determination within 90 days
  after the investigation begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall determine that a company is
  fulfilling its service obligations if it is satisfied that the company
  provides the highest level of service in respect of those obligations
  that it can reasonably provide in the circumstances, having regard to
  the following considerations:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the traffic to which the service obligations
    relate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reasonableness of the shipper's requests with
    respect to the traffic;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the service that the shipper requires with respect
    to the traffic;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any undertaking with respect to the traffic given
    by the shipper to the company;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the company's and the shipper's operational
    requirements and restrictions;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the company's obligations, if any, with respect to
    a public passenger service provider;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the company's obligations in respect of the
    operation of the railway under this Act;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the company's contingency plans to allow it to
    fulfil its service obligations when faced with foreseeable or
    cyclical events; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any information that the Agency considers relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential contract binding on Agency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a company and a shipper agree, by means of a
  confidential contract, on the manner in which service obligations
  under section 113 are to be fulfilled by the company, the terms of
  that agreement are binding on the Agency in making its determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Long-haul interswitching order binding
  on Agency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a long-haul interwitching order has been made
  under subsection 134(1), the terms established by the order that are
  related to the manner in which the local carrier is to fulfil its
  service obligations are binding on the Agency in making its
  determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders of Agency

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency determines that a company is not
  fulfilling any of its service obligations, the Agency may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} specific works be constructed or carried out,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} property be acquired,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} cars, motive power or other equipment be
      allotted, distributed, used or moved as specified by the Agency,
      or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any specified steps, systems or methods be taken
      or followed by the company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify in the order the maximum charges that may
    be made by the company in respect of the matter so ordered;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the company to fulfil that obligation in any
    manner and within any time or during any period that the Agency
    deems expedient, having regard to all proper interests, and specify
    the particulars of the obligation to be fulfilled;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} order the company to compensate any person
    adversely affected for any expenses that they incurred as a result
    of the company's failure to fulfil its service obligations or, if
    the company is a party to a confidential contract with a shipper
    that requires the company to pay an amount of compensation for
    expenses incurred by the shipper as a result of the company's
    failure to fulfil its service obligations, order the company to pay
    that amount to the shipper;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the service obligation is in respect of a
    grain-dependent branch line listed in Schedule I, order the company
    to add to the plan it is required to prepare under subsection 141(1)
    an indication that it intends to take steps to discontinue operating
    the line; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the service obligation is in respect of a
    grain-dependent branch line listed in Schedule I, order the company,
    on the terms and conditions that the Agency considers appropriate,
    to grant to another railway company the right

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to run and operate its trains over and on any
      portion of the line, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in so far as necessary to provide service to the
      line, to run and operate its trains over and on any portion of any
      other portion of the railway of the company against which the
      order is made but not to solicit traffic on that railway, to take
      possession of, use or occupy any land belonging to that company
      and to use the whole or any portion of that company's
      right-of-way, tracks, terminals, stations or station grounds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of action on default

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every person aggrieved by any neglect or refusal of a
  company to fulfil its service obligations has, subject to this Act, an
  action for the neglect or refusal against the company.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of action not limited

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} If an arbitrator's decision made under section
  169.37 includes a term with respect to an amount described in
  paragraph 169.31(1)(c.1), the term does not limit the right to claim
  an amount of compensation in an action under subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Company not relieved

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the terms of a confidential contract
  referred to in subsection 113(4) or a tariff that sets out, in
  accordance with subsection 136.4(1), terms established in a long-haul
  interswitching order, a company is not relieved from an action taken
  under subsection (5) by any notice, condition or declaration if the
  damage claimed in the action arises from any negligence or omission of
  the company or any of its employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 116
- 2000, c. 16, s. 4
- 2014, c. 8, s. 5.1
- 2018, c. 10, ss. 23, 95
:::

c. 8, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Tariffs --- General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55522 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rates to be charged

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 126, a railway
  company shall not charge a rate in respect of the movement of traffic
  or passengers unless the rate is set out in a tariff that has been
  issued and published in accordance with this Division and is in
  effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tariff to include prescribed
  information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The tariff must include any information that the
  Agency may prescribe by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility of tariff

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall make the tariff accessible
  to the public by publishing it on its Internet site.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 10, s. 24\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of tariff

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall keep a record of the tariff
  for at least three years after its cancellation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 117
- 2018, c. 10, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Tariffs --- Freight]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55535 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Freight tariff requested by shipper

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** A railway company
shall, at the request of a shipper, issue a tariff in respect of the
movement of traffic on its railway.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of change of tariff

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company that proposes to increase a rate in a tariff for the
  movement of traffic shall publish a notice of the increase at least 30
  days before its effective date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of freight tariff

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a railway company issues and publishes a tariff of
  rates for the movement of traffic in accordance with this Division and
  Division VI,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the rates are the lawful rates of the railway
    company and, subject to subsection (1), they take effect on the date
    stated in the tariff;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the tariff supersedes any preceding tariff or any
    portion of it in so far as any rate in the tariff is varied; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a railway company that owns or operates a railway
    line in respect of which the tariff is issued shall charge the rates
    in the tariff until they expire or until the tariff is superseded by
    a new tariff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 119
- 2008, c. 5, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to vessels

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** The provisions of this
Division relating to tariffs, in so far as the Agency considers them
applicable, apply to traffic carried by a railway company by water
between ports or places in Canada if the company owns, charters, uses,
maintains or operates vessels for carrying traffic by water between
ports or places in Canada, or is a party to an arrangement for using,
maintaining or operating vessels for that purpose.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unreasonable charges or terms

- **[[120.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on complaint in writing to the
  Agency by a shipper who is subject to any charges and associated terms
  and conditions for the movement of traffic or for the provision of
  incidental services that are found in a tariff that applies to more
  than one shipper other than a tariff referred to in subsection 165(3),
  the Agency finds that the charges or associated terms and conditions
  are unreasonable, the Agency may, by order, establish new charges or
  associated terms and conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of validity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) remains in effect
  for the period, not exceeding one year, specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether any charges or associated terms
  and conditions are unreasonable, the Agency shall take into account
  the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the objective of the charges or associated terms
    and conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the industry practice in setting the charges or
    associated terms and conditions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a complaint relating to the
    provision of any incidental service, the existence of an effective,
    adequate and competitive alternative to the provision of that
    service; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the Agency considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commercially fair and reasonable

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any charges or associated terms and conditions
  established by the Agency shall be commercially fair and reasonable to
  the shippers who are subject to them as well as to the railway company
  that issued the tariff containing them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to vary tariff

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall, without delay after the
  Agency establishes any charges or associated terms and conditions,
  vary its tariff to reflect those charges or associated terms and
  conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No variation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall not vary its tariff with
  respect to any charges or associated terms and conditions established
  by the Agency until the period referred to in subsection (2) has
  expired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, this section does not apply to
  rates for the movement of traffic.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 3
:::

#### [Joint Rates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55571 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous route in Canada

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  traffic is to move over a continuous route in Canada and portions of
  it are operated by two or more railway companies, the companies shall,
  at the request of a shipper intending to move the traffic,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} agree on a joint tariff for the continuous route
    and on the apportionment of the rate in the joint tariff; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} enter into a confidential contract for the
    continuous route.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency may decide if no agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the railway companies fail to agree or to enter
  into a confidential contract, the Agency, on the application of the
  shipper, may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct the companies, within any time that the
    Agency may specify, to agree on a joint tariff for the continuous
    route and an apportionment of the rate that is satisfactory to the
    Agency; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within ninety days after the application is
    received by the Agency,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} determine the route and the rate and apportion
      the rate among the companies, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} determine the dates, not earlier than the date
      of receipt by the Agency of the application, when the rate comes
      into effect and when it must be published.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refund to shipper

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency determines a rate under paragraph
  (2)(b), the companies that operate the route shall pay a shipper who
  moved traffic over the route an amount equal to the difference, if
  any, between the rate that was paid by the shipper and the rate
  determined by the Agency, applicable to all movements of traffic by
  the shipper over the route from the date on which the application was
  made to the date on which the determined rate comes into effect.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication requirements for joint tariff
or rate

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  joint tariff or rate is agreed on or determined under section 121, the
  requirements of this Division for the publication of the tariff or
  rate apply only to the railway company operating the railway line on
  which the movement of the traffic originates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of subsection 121(2) tariff
  or rate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A joint tariff or rate agreed on or determined under
  subsection 121(2) must be published without delay, or within any
  additional time that the Agency may specify.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of joint tariff --- from
Canada to or through foreign country

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** If traffic is to move
over a continuous route from a point in Canada through a foreign country
into Canada, or from a point in Canada to a foreign country, and the
continuous route is operated by two or more railway companies, the
requirements of this Division for the publication of an applicable joint
tariff apply only to the railway company operating the railway line on
which the movement of the traffic originates.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of joint tariff --- from
foreign country to or through Canada

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** If traffic is to move
over a continuous route from a point in a foreign country into Canada,
or from a foreign country through Canada into a foreign country, and the
continuous route is operated by two or more companies, the requirements
of this Division for the publication of an applicable joint tariff apply
only to the railway company operating the railway line on which the
traffic is first moved in Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous carriage not to be prevented

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  railway company shall, by any combination, contract or agreement,
  express or implied, or by any other means, prevent traffic from being
  moved on a continuous route from the point of origin to the point of
  destination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Break in bulk, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A break in bulk, stop or interruption made by a
  railway company does not prevent the movement of traffic from being
  treated, for the purposes of sections 121 to 124, as one continuous
  movement from the point of origin to the point of destination, if the
  break, stop or interruption was made in good faith for a necessary
  purpose and without intent to avoid or unnecessarily interrupt the
  continuous movement or to evade the provisions of this Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency may require information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may direct a railway company operating on
  a continuous route to inform the Agency of the proportion of the rates
  that the railway company, or any other company operating on the
  continuous route, is to receive or has received under a joint tariff
  or confidential contract applicable to the route.

#### [Confidential Contracts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55601 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential contracts

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company may enter into a contract with a shipper that the
  parties agree to keep confidential respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the rates to be charged by the company to the
    shipper;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reductions or allowances pertaining to rates in
    tariffs that have been issued and published in accordance with this
    Division;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} rebates or allowances pertaining to rates in
    tariffs or confidential contracts that have previously been lawfully
    charged;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the company is to fulfil its
    service obligations under section 113; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any conditions relating to the traffic to be moved
    by the company, including any amount to be paid by the company or
    the shipper in relation to a failure to comply with any condition
    related to the service obligations referred to in paragraph (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidential contract

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A shipper may request that a railway company make
  it an offer to enter into a contract under subsection (1) with the
  railway company respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the company is to fulfil its
    service obligations under section 113; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any amount to be paid in relation to the company's
    or the shipper's failure to comply with a term related to those
    service obligations, the purpose of which is to encourage the
    efficient movement of the shipper's traffic and the performance of
    the railway system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(1.11)]{.lawlabel} The shipper may only make a request in respect of
  an amount described in paragraph (1.1)(b) if the amount relates to a
  term that is included in the request under subsection (1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of request

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The request must describe the traffic to which it
  relates, the services requested by the shipper with respect to the
  traffic and any undertaking that the shipper is prepared to give to
  the railway company with respect to the traffic or services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The railway company must make its offer within 30
  days after the day on which it receives the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception to offer

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1.5), the railway company is
  not required to include in its offer terms with respect to a matter
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is governed by a written agreement to which the
    shipper and the railway company are parties;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of an order, other than an interim
    order, made under subsection 116(4);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is set out in a tariff referred to in subsection
    136.4(1) or 165(3); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of an arbitration decision made
    under section 169.37.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} The railway company must include in its offer terms
  with respect to a matter that is governed by an agreement, the subject
  of an order or decision or set out in a tariff, referred to in
  subsection (1.4) if the agreement, order, decision or tariff expires
  within two months after the day on which the railway company receives
  the request referred to in subsection (1.1). The terms must apply to a
  period that begins after the agreement, order, decision or tariff
  expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No investigation or arbitration of
  confidential contracts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No party to a confidential contract is entitled to
  submit a matter governed by the contract to the Agency for final offer
  arbitration under section 161, without the consent of all the parties
  to the contract.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 126
- 2013, c. 31, s. 8
- 2018, c. 10, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Interswitching]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55632 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to interswitch traffic
between connecting lines

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  railway line of one railway company connects with a railway line of
  another railway company, an application for an interswitching order
  may be made to the Agency by either company, by a municipal government
  or by any other interested person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the point of origin or destination of a continuous
  movement of traffic is within a radius of 30 km of an interchange, the
  Agency may order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one of the companies to interswitch the traffic;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the railway companies to provide reasonable
    facilities for the convenient interswitching of traffic in both
    directions at an interchange between the lines of either railway and
    those of other railway companies connecting with them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- Prairies

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If the point of origin or destination of a
  continuous movement of traffic is in whole or in part in Manitoba,
  Saskatchewan or Alberta and is located within a radius of 160 km of an
  interchange that is in whole or in part in Manitoba, Saskatchewan or
  Alberta but outside a radius of 30 km of the interchange, the Agency
  may order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one of the companies to interswitch the traffic;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the railway companies to provide reasonable
    facilities for the convenient interswitching of traffic in both
    directions at an interchange between the lines of either railway and
    those of other railway companies connecting with them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interswitching limits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the point of origin or destination of a continuous
  movement of traffic is within a radius of 30 km of an interchange, a
  railway company shall not transfer the traffic at the interchange
  except in accordance with the regulations and the interswitching rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of interswitching limits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On the application of a person referred to in
  subsection (1), the Agency may deem a point of origin or destination
  of a movement of traffic in any particular case to be within 30 km of
  an interchange if the Agency is of the opinion that, in the
  circumstances, the point of origin or destination is reasonably close
  to the interchange.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interswitching limits --- Prairies

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the point of origin or destination of a continuous
  movement of traffic is in whole or in part in Manitoba, Saskatchewan
  or Alberta and is located within a radius of 160 km of an interchange
  that is in whole or in part in Manitoba, Saskatchewan or Alberta but
  outside a radius of 30 km of the interchange, a railway company must
  not transfer the traffic at the interchange except in accordance with
  the regulations and the interswitching rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information --- traffic

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} When providing the Minister with information under
  regulations made under paragraph 50(1.01)(a), the Canadian National
  Railway Company and the Canadian Pacific Railway Company are also
  required to provide the Minister, in the same form and manner, with
  the following information with respect to any traffic that is moved by
  a railway car in order to permit the Minister to assess the effects of
  the application of subsections (2.1) and (5):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an indication as to whether the point of origin or
    destination of the movement of the railway car was located within a
    radius of 30 km of an interchange that is in whole or in part in
    Manitoba, Saskatchewan or Alberta;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an indication as to whether the point of origin or
    destination of the movement of the railway car was within a radius
    of 160 km of an interchange that is in whole or in part in Manitoba,
    Saskatchewan or Alberta but outside a radius of 30 km of the
    interchange;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an indication as to whether the railway car was
    moved by the railway company at the interswitching rate; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if possible, an indication as to whether the
    railway car was moved by another railway company at the
    interswitching rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On request, a railway company must provide to the
  Minister, in the form and manner specified by the Minister, any of the
  information or documents that have been provided to the Agency under
  section 128.1 in order to permit the Minister to assess the effects of
  the application of subsections (2.1) and (5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 127
- 2018, c. 10, ss. 26, 95
- [2023, c. 26, s. 443](#1410605-1420745){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410605-1420745.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interswitching rate

- **[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, no later than
  December 1 of every year, determine the rate per car to be charged for
  interswitching traffic for the following calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interswitching rate --- Prairies

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, no later than 90 days after the
  day on which this subsection comes into force, determine the rate per
  car to be charged for interswitching traffic within a zone that
  includes a point of origin or destination of a continuous movement of
  traffic that is located in whole or in part in Manitoba, Saskatchewan
  or Alberta and is within a radius of 160 km of an interchange that is
  in whole or in part in Manitoba, Saskatchewan or Alberta but outside a
  radius of 30 km of the interchange, for the calendar year in which
  this subsection comes into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining an interswitching rate, the Agency
  shall take into consideration

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any reduction in costs that, in the opinion of the
    Agency, results from moving a greater number of cars or from
    transferring several cars at the same time; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any long-term investment needed in the railways.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit on rate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining an interswitching rate, the Agency
  shall consider the average variable costs of all movements of traffic
  that are subject to the rate and the rate shall not be less than the
  variable costs of moving the traffic, as determined by the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of method

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, when it makes its determination
  under subsection (1), publish the method that it followed for
  determining the rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of method --- subsection
  (1.1)

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, when it makes its determination
  under subsection (1.1), publish the method that it followed for
  determining the rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interswitching rate to be published

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall cause the interswitching rate to be
  published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) no later
  than December 31 before the beginning of the calendar year for which
  the rate applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication --- subsection (1.1)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall, no later than 90 days after the day
  on which this subsection comes into force, publish the rate determined
  under subsection (1.1) on its Internet site.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 27
- [2023, c. 26, s. 444](#1410610-1414086){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410610-1414086.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127.1-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 127.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repeal

**[[127.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.2 .sectionLabel}** This section and
