subsections 127(2.1) and (5) to (7) and 127.1(1.1), (4.1) and (6) are
repealed on the day that, in the 18th month after the month in which
subsection 127(2.1) comes into force, has the same calendar number as
the day on which that subsection 127(2.1) comes into force or, if that
18th month has no day with that number, the last day of that 18th month.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 445](#1410619-1420749){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410619-1420749.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing terms and conditions governing the
    interswitching of traffic, other than terms and conditions relating
    to safety; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establishing distance zones for the purpose of
    determining the interswitching rate.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 10, s. 28\]]{.Repealed}

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 8, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2) and (3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 10, s. 28\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of lines does not affect
  entitlement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the transfer of a railway
  line, or an operating interest in it, under Division V or section 158
  of the National Transportation Act, 1987 does not affect any
  entitlement to an interswitching rate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of interswitching regulations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall review the regulations when the
  circumstances warrant and at least once in every five year period
  after the regulations are made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 128
- 2014, c. 8, s. 7
- 2018, c. 10, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-128-20170801.html "Link to previous version of section 128"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to require information

**[[128.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128.1 .sectionLabel}** No later than
August 31 of every year, a railway company shall provide to the Agency,
in the form and manner specified by the Agency, the information or
documents that the Agency considers necessary to exercise its powers or
perform its duties or functions under section 127.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

#### [Long-haul Interswitching]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55679 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Long-haul interswitching order

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  shipper may apply to the Agency for a long-haul interswitching order
  against a railway company that is a class 1 rail carrier if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the shipper has access to the lines of only that
    railway company at the point of origin or destination of the
    movement of the shipper's traffic in the reasonable direction of the
    traffic and its destination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a continuous route between those points is operated
    by two or more railway companies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the shipper is dissatisfied with a rate charged or
    proposed to be charged by the railway company referred to in
    paragraph (a) or with the proposed manner in which that railway
    company is to fulfil its service obligations for the movement of the
    shipper's traffic between the point of origin or destination that is
    served exclusively by that railway company and the nearest
    interchange in Canada with a connecting carrier; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the matter described in paragraph (c) cannot be
    resolved between the shipper and the railway company.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, at both the point of origin and the point of
  destination of the movement of the shipper's traffic, a shipper has
  access to the lines of only one railway company that is a class 1 rail
  carrier, the shipper is entitled to apply for only one long-haul
  interswitching order, which is to be in respect of either the movement
  of the traffic from the point of origin to the nearest interchange in
  Canada or the movement of the traffic from the nearest interchange in
  Canada to the point of destination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No entitlement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A shipper is not entitled to apply to the Agency for
  a long-haul interswitching order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the point of origin or destination that is
    served exclusively by the local carrier is within a radius of 30 km
    of an interchange in Canada that is in the reasonable direction of
    the shipper's traffic and its destination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the point of origin or destination that is
    served exclusively by the local carrier or the nearest interchange
    is located within the Quebec--Windsor corridor or the
    Vancouver--Kamloops corridor;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the point of origin or destination that is
    served exclusively by the local carrier is located on a track that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} serves a reload or distribution compound, a
      container terminal or any other facility operated by the local
      carrier or for the local carrier's own purposes, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is used by the local carrier for the transfer of
      traffic between cars or between a car and a warehouse owned by the
      local carrier;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for the movement of vehicles, as defined in section
    2 of the [Motor Vehicle Safety Act](/eng/acts/M-10.01), or of parts
    of those vehicles;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for the movement of TIH (Toxic Inhalation Hazard)
    material;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for the movement of radioactive material;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} for the movement of oversized traffic on flat cars,
    if the dimensions of the traffic require exceptional measures be
    taken;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for the movement, on flat cars, of containers or
    trailers;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the traffic to be moved is already the subject
    of a long-haul interswitching order;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} if an order or consent agreement made under Part
    VIII of the [Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34), which followed an
    application made by the Commissioner of Competition, addresses the
    rate for the traffic to be moved; or

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} in any other case specified in the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming --- interchange

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (3)(b), an interchange
  located in the metropolitan area of Montreal is deemed to be the
  nearest interchange and to be located outside the Quebec--Windsor
  corridor if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the point of origin of the movement of the
    shipper's traffic is located in Quebec and north of the
    Quebec-Windsor corridor;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the shipper has access to the lines of only one
    class 1 rail carrier at the point of origin; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nearest interchange is located in the
    Quebec--Windsor corridor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 129
- 2018, c. 10, ss. 29, 95
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-129-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 129"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shipper has access to the lines of only one
    railway company at the point of origin or destination of the
    movement of the shipper's traffic;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the railway company referred to in paragraph (a) is
    not a class 1 rail carrier; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a junction between the lines of the
    railway company referred to in paragraph (a) and the lines of a
    railway that is operated by a class 1 rail carrier and the shipper
    has access to only those railway lines at that junction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of sections 129 and 131 to 136.6,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the junction referred to in paragraph (1)(c) is
    deemed to be the point of origin or the point of destination, as the
    case may be;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the junction referred to in paragraph (1)(c) is
    deemed to be served exclusively by the class 1 rail carrier referred
    to in that paragraph; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the class 1 rail carrier referred to in paragraph
    (1)(c) is deemed to be the local carrier.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 130
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transferred railway lines

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
the transfer of a railway line, or an operating interest in it, under
Division V or section 158 of the National Transportation Act, 1987 does
not affect the right of a shipper to apply for a long-haul
interswitching order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 131
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** The shipper shall, in
its application for a long-haul interswitching order,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide an undertaking to the local carrier to move
  the traffic by rail with the local carrier between the point of origin
  or destination that is served exclusively by the local carrier and the
  nearest interchange in Canada with a connecting carrier in accordance
  with the long-haul interswitching order; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} indicate the continuous route that the shipper has
  chosen for the movement of the shipper's traffic.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 132
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-132-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 132"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal of application

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** The Agency shall
dismiss the application for a long-haul interswitching order if the
shipper does not demonstrate, to the Agency's satisfaction, that an
attempt has been made to resolve the matters referred to in the
application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 133
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-133-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 133"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination by Agency

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 business days after
  receiving the application for a long-haul interswitching order, the
  Agency shall, by order, determine any of the following matters in
  respect of which the shipper and the local carrier do not agree:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the long-haul interswitching rate that applies in
    respect of the movement of the shipper's traffic between the point
    of origin or destination that is served exclusively by the class 1
    rail carrier and the nearest interchange in Canada with a connecting
    carrier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the continuous route from the point of origin to
    the point of destination;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nearest interchange in Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the local carrier is to fulfil
    its service obligations in respect of the movement of traffic
    described in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum portion of traffic

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall not make a long-haul interswitching
  order if the movement of the shipper's traffic between the point of
  origin or destination that is served exclusively by the class 1 rail
  carrier and the nearest interchange in Canada exceeds the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 1 200 km; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 50% of the total number of kilometres over which
    the traffic is moved by rail in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 134
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-134-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 134"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Long-haul interswitching rate

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following rules apply to the determination of the long-haul
  interswitching rate:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the first 30 km, the rate is to be the
    interswitching rate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the remainder of the distance, the Agency shall
    determine the rate by having regard to the revenue per tonne
    kilometre for the movement by the local carrier of comparable
    traffic in respect of which no long-haul interswitching rate
    applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum rate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall not determine the rate described in
  paragraph (1)(b) to be less than the average of the revenue per tonne
  kilometre for the movement by the local carrier of comparable traffic
  in respect of which no long-haul interswitching rate applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider --- comparable
  traffic

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(b), the Agency shall
  determine what constitutes comparable traffic by having regard to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of traffic;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the distance over which the traffic is moved;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the conditions of the movement of the traffic,
    including whether it is moved as single cars, blocks of cars or unit
    trains;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the type and ownership of the cars used to move the
    traffic;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the handling requirements for the traffic;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the volume and frequency of the traffic;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any undertaking given by the shipper in respect of
    the volume of the traffic;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any incentives, rebates or any similar reductions
    in respect of the traffic; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any other factor related to the requirements of the
    shipper and the local carrier that the Agency considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider --- rate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall determine the rate described in
  paragraph (1)(b) by having regard to the factors described in
  subsection (3), the density of traffic on the lines of the local
  carrier on which the traffic is to be moved and any long-term
  investment needed in those lines.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 135
- 2018, c. 10, ss. 29, 95
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-135-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 135"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous route

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency shall, in determining the continuous route from the point of
  origin to the point of destination, have regard to the continuous
  route that has been chosen by the shipper for the movement of its
  traffic in its application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Route in Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the point of destination of the continuous route
  of a movement of the shipper's traffic is in Canada, the Agency shall
  determine a continuous route that is wholly within Canada, unless
  there is no cost-effective continuous route wholly within Canada that
  is available to the shipper and over which it is reasonable to move
  the shipper's traffic.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export and import

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (1) and (2),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the traffic is to be moved through a port in
    Canada for import into Canada, that port is the point of origin; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the traffic is to be moved through a port in
    Canada for export out of Canada, that port is the point of
    destination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 136
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-136-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 136"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nearest interchange

**[[136.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.1 .sectionLabel}** The Agency shall
determine the nearest interchange in Canada to be the one nearest to the
point of origin or destination, whichever is served exclusively by the
local carrier, in the reasonable direction of the movement of the
traffic from the point of origin to the point of destination on the
continuous route, unless the local carrier can demonstrate that the
interchange cannot be used for engineering reasons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service obligations

**[[136.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.2 .sectionLabel}** The Agency shall
determine the manner in which the local carrier is to fulfil its service
obligations by having regard to the considerations set out in paragraphs
116(1.2)(a) to (i).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

**[[136.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.3 .sectionLabel}** The long-haul
interswitching order applies to the parties for a period of one year as
of the date of the order, unless the parties agree otherwise.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation in tariff

- **[[136.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The local carrier shall, without
  delay after the long-haul interswitching order is made, set out in a
  tariff the terms established by the order, unless the shipper and
  local carrier agree to include those terms in a confidential contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication not required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 117(3) does not apply in respect of the
  tariff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No final offer arbitration

**[[136.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.5 .sectionLabel}** If a long-haul
interswitching order is made by the Agency, the shipper is not entitled
to submit any matter related to the movement of the traffic that is the
subject of the order to the Agency for final offer arbitration under
section 161.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation of carriers to provide cars

- **[[136.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any agreement to the
  contrary, if a long-haul interswitching order is made, the connecting
  carrier is responsible for providing the shipper with an adequate
  supply of cars for the traffic being moved, in addition to its other
  service obligations in relation to the movement of the traffic.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional obligations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any agreement to the contrary, the
  connecting carrier is, in respect of the interchange referred to in
  paragraph 129(1)(c), responsible for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a prorated share, determined in accordance with
    subsection (3), of the costs of operating and maintaining the
    interchange during the period in which the long-haul interswitching
    order applies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the capital cost of making any change to the
    interchange that may be necessary for transferring the traffic that
    is the subject of the long-haul interswitching order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of prorated share

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The prorated share is the proportion that the traffic
  that is the subject of the order transferred at the interchange and
  moved by the connecting carrier during the period in which the order
  applies is of the total traffic transferred at the interchange during
  that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Share of capital cost

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If more than one connecting carrier moves the traffic
  that is the subject of the order, the capital cost is to be shared
  between the connecting carriers based on each connecting carrier's
  share of the amount of traffic moved.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[136.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.7 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, for the purpose of paragraph 129(3)(k), make regulations
specifying cases in which a shipper is not entitled to apply for a
long-haul interswitching order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of operation

**[[136.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.8 .sectionLabel}** If the Governor in
Council is of the opinion that the financial viability of a railway
company is seriously affected by the operation of sections 129 to 136.7,
the Governor in Council may, by order, suspend the operation of those
sections during the period specified in the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

#### [Interchanges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55828 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List

- **[[136.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A railway company shall prepare and
  keep up to date a list of the locations of the interchanges on the
  railway that the company operates. It shall publish the list on its
  Internet site or the Internet site of an association or other entity
  representing railway companies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A railway company may remove an interchange from its
  list only after the expiry of 120 days after it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has published a notice of its intention to do so on
    its Internet site or the Internet site of an association or other
    entity representing railway companies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has sent a copy of the notice to the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service obligations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the removal of an interchange
  under subsection (2) does not relieve a railway company from its
  service obligations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 29
:::

#### [Means to Deal with Carriers' Liability]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55840 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  issue related to liability, including liability to a third party, in
  respect of the movement of a shipper's traffic shall be dealt with
  between the railway company and the shipper only by means of a written
  agreement that is signed by the shipper or by an association or other
  entity representing shippers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is no agreement, the railway company's
  liability to the shipper in respect of a loss of or damage to a
  shipper's traffic in the company's possession or for any delay in its
  movement shall be dealt with between the company and the shipper,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the application of the company, by the Agency;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there is no application or, if there is an
    application but the Agency does not specify any terms or conditions
    with respect to the matter, in the manner set out in the
    regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(b), the Agency may
  make regulations respecting the manner in which a railway company's
  liability under subsection (2) is to be dealt with between a company
  and a shipper.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 137
- 2015, c. 31, s. 9
- 2018, c. 10, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20150618.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints

**[[137.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137.1 .sectionLabel}** If, after receiving
a complaint, the Agency finds that a railway company is not complying
with subsection 137(1), the Agency may order it to take any measures
that the Agency considers appropriate to comply with that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 31
:::

#### [Running Rights and Joint Track Usage]{.HTitleText3} {#h-55856 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application by railway company

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company may apply to the Agency for the right to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take possession of, use or occupy any land
    belonging to any other railway company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use the whole or any portion of the right-of-way,
    tracks, terminals, stations or station grounds of any other railway
    company; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} run and operate its trains over and on any portion
    of the railway of any other railway company.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application may be granted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may grant the right and may make any order
  and impose any conditions on either railway company respecting the
  exercise or restriction of the rights as appear just or desirable to
  the Agency, having regard to the public interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall pay compensation to the
  other railway company for the right granted and, if they do not agree
  on the compensation, the Agency may, by order, fix the amount to be
  paid.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for joint or common use of
right-of-way

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the application of a railway company, a
    municipal government or any other interested person, or on the
    Governor in Council's own initiative, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after any investigation that the Governor in
    Council considers necessary,

  request two or more railway companies to consider the joint or common
  use of a right-of-way if the Governor in Council is of the opinion
  that its joint or common use may improve the efficiency and
  effectiveness of rail transport and would not unduly impair the
  commercial interests of the companies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order in Council for joint or common
  use of right-of-way

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council is satisfied that
  significant efficiencies and cost savings would result from joint or
  common use of the right-of-way by two or more railway companies and
  would not unduly impair the commercial interests of the companies, the
  Governor in Council may make any order for the joint or common use of
  the right-of-way that the Governor in Council considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may also, by order, fix the
  amount of compensation to be paid in respect of the joint or common
  use of the right-of-way and any related work if the companies do not
  agree on the amount of that compensation.

### [DIVISION V]{.HLabel2}[Transferring and Discontinuing the Operation of Railway Lines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-55877 .Subheading}

Definition of [[railway line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Division, [[railway line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a portion
  of a railway line, but does not include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a yard track, siding or spur; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} other track auxiliary to a railway line.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may determine as a question of fact what
  constitutes a yard track, siding, spur or other track auxiliary to a
  railway line.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Three-year plan

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company shall prepare and keep up to date a plan indicating
  for each of its railway lines whether it intends to continue to
  operate the line or whether, within the next three years, it intends
  to take steps to discontinue operating the line.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public accessibility of plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall make the plan accessible to
  the public by publishing it on its Internet site or the Internet site
  of an association or other entity representing railway companies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of changes

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Whenever the railway company makes a change to the
  plan, it shall notify the following of the change within 10 days after
  the change:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Agency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the minister responsible for transportation matters
    in the government of each province through which the railway line
    passes;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the chairperson of every urban transit authority
    through whose territory the railway line passes; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the clerk or other senior administrative officer of
    every municipal or district government through which the railway
    line passes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to provide to Minister

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall, within 60 days after
  indicating in the plan its intention to discontinue operating a
  railway line, provide to the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an assessment of whether or not section 96 applies
    to the land on which the railway line is located; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a legal description of any land to which the
    assessment indicates section 96 applies and, in the form specified
    by the Minister, geographical information that would allow for
    mapping of the land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuance already indicated in
  plan

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} If a railway company has, on the day on which
  subsection (2.2) comes into force, a plan indicating its intention to
  discontinue operating a railway line, but the company has not yet made
  an advertisement under section 143 in respect of that line, it shall
  provide to the Minister the information referred to in that subsection
  before making the advertisement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When sale, etc., permitted

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 144.1, a railway company may sell,
  lease or otherwise transfer its railway lines, or its operating
  interest in its lines, for continued operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall provide a written
  declaration to the person to whom the railway line or the operating
  interest is being sold, leased or otherwise transferred, stating that
  the sale, lease or transfer is in compliance with section 96. It shall
  also send a copy of the declaration to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued operation of a portion of a
  line

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A railway company that sells, leases or otherwise
  transfers a portion of a grain-dependent branch line listed in
  Schedule I, or its operating interest in such a portion, to a person
  who intends to operate the portion shall continue to operate the
  remaining portion for three years, unless the Minister determines that
  it is not in the public interest for the company to do so.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 141
- 2000, c. 16, s. 5
- 2007, c. 19, s. 35
- 2018, c. 10, s. 32
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with steps for discontinuance

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  railway company shall comply with the steps described in this Division
  before discontinuing operating a railway line. The railway company
  shall publish and keep up to date on its Internet site or the Internet
  site of an association or other entity representing railway companies
  a report that sets out the date that it commenced and completed each
  step.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A railway company shall not take steps to discontinue
  operating a railway line before the company's intention to discontinue
  operating the line has been indicated in its plan for at least 12
  months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply to a railway company
  that is the subject of proceedings under the [Companies' Creditors
  Arrangement Act](/eng/acts/C-36) or the [Bankruptcy and Insolvency
  Act](/eng/acts/B-3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Community-based groups

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply and a railway company
  shall without delay take the steps described in section 143 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal government, a provincial, municipal or
    district government or a community-based group endorsed in writing
    by such a government has written to the company to express an
    interest in acquiring all or a portion of a grain-dependent branch
    line that is listed in Schedule I for the purpose of continuing to
    operate that line or portion of a line; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that line or portion of a line is indicated on the
    company's plan as being a line or a portion of a line that the
    company intends to take steps to discontinue operating.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 142
- 2000, c. 16, s. 6
- 2018, c. 10, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advertisement of availability of railway
line for continued rail operations

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  railway company shall advertise the availability of the railway line,
  or any operating interest that the company has in it, for sale, lease
  or other transfer for continued operation and its intention to
  discontinue operating the line if it is not transferred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of advertisement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The advertisement must include a description of the
  railway line and how it or the operating interest is to be
  transferred, whether by sale, lease or otherwise, and an outline of
  the steps that must be taken before the operation of the line may be
  discontinued, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the advertisement is directed to
    persons interested in buying, leasing or otherwise acquiring the
    railway line, or the railway company's operating interest in it, for
    the purpose of continuing railway operations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date by which interested persons must make
    their interest known in writing to the company, but that date must
    be at least sixty days after the first publication of the
    advertisement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure --- advertisement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The advertisement shall also disclose

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the existence of any agreement between the railway
    company and a public passenger service provider in respect of the
    operation of a passenger rail service on the railway line; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an indication as to whether or not section 96
    applies to the land on which that railway line is located.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2007, c. 19, s. 36\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 143
- 2007, c. 19, s. 36
- 2018, c. 10, s. 34
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-143-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 143"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of process

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  railway company shall disclose the process it intends to follow for
  receiving and evaluating offers to each interested person who makes
  their interest known in accordance with the advertisement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2007, c. 19, s. 37\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negotiation in good faith

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall negotiate with an
  interested person in good faith and in accordance with the process it
  discloses and the interested person shall negotiate with the company
  in good faith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Net salvage value

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, on application by a party to a
  negotiation, determine the net salvage value of the railway line and
  may, if it is of the opinion that the railway company has removed any
  of the infrastructure associated with the line in order to reduce
  traffic on the line, deduct from the net salvage value the amount that
  the Agency determines is the cost of replacing the removed
  infrastructure. The party who made the application shall reimburse the
  Agency its costs associated with the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The railway company has six months to reach an
  agreement after the final date stated in the advertisement for persons
  to make their interest known.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision to continue operating a
  railway line

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an agreement is not reached within the six months,
  the railway company may decide to continue operating the railway line,
  in which case it is not required to comply with section 145, but shall
  amend its plan to reflect its decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} If an agreement is reached, including an agreement
  entered into to in accordance with an order by the Agency, the railway
  company shall provide a written declaration to the person to whom the
  railway line or the operating interest is being sold, leased or
  otherwise transferred, stating that the sale, lease or transfer is in
  compliance with section 96. It shall also send a copy of the
  declaration to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedy if bad faith by a railway
  company

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If, on complaint in writing by the interested person,
  the Agency finds that the railway company is not negotiating in good
  faith and the Agency considers that a sale, lease or other transfer of
  the railway line, or the company's operating interest in the line, to
  the interested person for continued operation would be commercially
  fair and reasonable to the parties, the Agency may order the railway
  company to enter into an agreement with the interested person to
  effect the transfer and with respect to operating arrangements for the
  interchange of traffic, subject to the terms and conditions, including
  consideration, specified by the Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedy if bad faith by an interested
  person

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If, on complaint in writing by the railway company,
  the Agency finds that the interested person is not negotiating in good
  faith, the Agency may order that the railway company is no longer
  required to negotiate with the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 144
- 2000, c. 16, s. 7
- 2007, c. 19, s. 37
- 2018, c. 10, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-144-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 144"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights and obligations under passenger
service agreements continued

- **[[144.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a railway line, or a railway
  company's operating interest in a railway line, is sold, leased or
  otherwise transferred under subsection 141(3) or as the result of an
  advertisement under subsection 143(1) and, before the day such
  advertisement was made, an agreement was in force between the railway
  company and a public passenger service provider in respect of the
  operation of a passenger rail service on the railway line, the rights
  and obligations of the railway company under the agreement in respect
  of the operation of that service on that line vest, as of the day the
  transfer takes place, in the person or entity to which the railway
  line, or the operating interest, is transferred, unless the public
  passenger service provider indicates otherwise before that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration that line is for general
  advantage of Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Whenever a railway company's rights and obligations
  under an agreement with VIA Rail Canada Inc. are vested in another
  person or entity by subsection (1), the portion of the railway line to
  which the agreement relates is hereby declared, as of the day the
  transfer takes place, to be a work for the general advantage of
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of declaration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The declaration referred to in subsection (2) ceases
  to have effect if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} VIA Rail Canada Inc. ceases to operate a passenger
    rail service on the portion of railway line to which the declaration
    relates; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operation of the railway line is discontinued.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 38
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer to governments

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  railway company shall offer to transfer all of its interest in the
  railway line to the governments and urban transit authorities
  mentioned in this section for not more than its net salvage value to
  be used for any purpose if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no person makes their interest known to the railway
    company, or no agreement with an interested person is reached,
    within the required time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an agreement is reached within the required time,
    but the transfer is not completed in accordance with the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure --- offer

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The offer shall disclose whether or not section 96
  applies to the land on which that railway line is located, and if the
  information described in paragraphs 141(2.2)(a) and (b) has not yet
  been provided to the Minister, the railway company shall provide it to
  the Minister with the offer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Which governments receive offer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After the requirement to make the offer arises, the
  railway company shall send it simultaneously

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the Minister if the railway line passes through

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than one province or outside Canada,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} land that is or was a reserve, as defined in
      subsection 2(1) of the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} land that is the subject of an agreement
      entered into by the railway company and the Minister for the
      settlement of aboriginal land claims, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a metropolitan area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the minister responsible for transportation
    matters in the government of each province through which the railway
    line passes;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to the chairperson of every urban transit authority
    through whose territory the railway line passes; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to the clerk or other senior administrative officer
    of every municipal or district government through whose territory
    the railway line passes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits for acceptance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 146.3(3), after the offer is
  received

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by the Minister, the Government of Canada may
    accept it within 60 days or, if the Minister has extended the period
    under subsection (3.1), within that period;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by a provincial minister, the government of the
    province may accept it within thirty days, unless the offer is
    received by the Minister, in which case the government of each
    province may accept it within an additional thirty days after the
    end of the period mentioned in paragraph (a) if it is not accepted
    under that paragraph;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} by an urban transit authority, it may accept it
    within an additional 30 days after the end of the period or periods
    for acceptance under paragraphs (a) and (b), if it is not accepted
    under those paragraphs; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by a municipal or district government, it may
    accept it within an additional 30 days after the end of the period
    or periods for acceptance under paragraphs (a), (b) and (b.1), if it
    is not accepted under those paragraphs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister considers it appropriate to do so,
  the Minister may extend the period referred to in paragraph (3)(a) by
  120 days. The Minister may further extend the period, but the total of
  those further extensions may not exceed 365 days. Each time the
  Minister extends the period, the Minister shall provide a notice to
  the railway company and the railway company shall notify the other
  governments and urban transit authorities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service obligations

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister extends the period referred to in
  paragraph (3)(a), the railway company has no service obligations in
  respect of the operation of the railway line commencing on the expiry
  of 150 days after the offer was received by the Minister and ending on
  the expiry of 280 days after the expiry of the extended period
  referred to in that paragraph. The railway company shall not remove
  any of the infrastructure associated with the line during the period
  for which it has no service obligations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication and notice of acceptance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Once a government or an urban transit authority
  communicates its written acceptance of the offer to the railway
  company, the right of any other government or urban transit authority
  to accept the offer is extinguished, and the railway company must
  notify the other governments and urban transit authorities of the
  acceptance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall, at the time of a
  transfer to a government or an urban transit authority, provide a
  written declaration to the government or urban transit authority
  stating that the transfer is in compliance with section 96. It shall
  also send a copy of the declaration to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Net salvage value

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a government or an urban transit authority accepts
  the offer, but cannot agree with the railway company on the net
  salvage value within 90 days after the acceptance, the Agency may, on
  the application of the government or urban transit authority or the
  railway company, determine the net salvage value.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 145
- 2007, c. 19, s. 39
- 2018, c. 10, s. 36
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuation

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  railway company has complied with the process set out in sections 143
  to 145, but an agreement for the sale, lease or other transfer of the
  railway line or an interest in it is not entered into through that
  process, the railway company may discontinue operating the line on
  providing notice of the discontinuance to the Agency. After providing
  the notice, the railway company has no obligations under this Act in
  respect of the operation of the railway line and has no obligations
  with respect to any operations by any public passenger service
  provider over the railway line.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents to accompany notice of
  discontinuance

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The notice of discontinuance shall be accompanied
  by a copy of the advertisement referred to in section 143 and the
  offers to the governments and transit authorities referred to in
  subsection 145(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the railway line, or any interest of the railway
  company in it, is sold, leased or otherwise transferred by an
  agreement entered into through the process set out in sections 143 to
  145 or otherwise, the railway company that conveyed the railway line
  has no obligations under this Act in respect of the operation of the
  railway line as and from the date the sale, lease or other transfer
  was completed and has no obligations with respect to any operations by
  any public passenger service provider over the railway line as and
  from that date.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 146
- 2007, c. 19, s. 40
- 2018, c. 10, s. 37
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation following return

- **[[146.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, by reason of the instrument or
  act by which a railway line or an operating interest in a railway line
  is transferred through the process set out in sections 143 to 145 or
  otherwise, the railway line or operating interest in the railway line
  returns to the railway company that transferred it, the railway
  company shall, within 60 days after the day on which the return takes
  place, resume operations of the line or follow the process set out in
  sections 143 to 145.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No condition or obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a railway line or operating interest in a railway
  line returns to a railway company that transferred it and the company
  decides to follow the process set out in sections 143 to 145 in
  respect of the railway line or operating interest, the company is not
  subject to subsection 142(2) in respect of the railway line or
  operating interest and has no obligations under this Act in respect of
  the operation of the railway line.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[146.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.02 .sectionLabel}** Despite section
146.01, if a railway line or operating interest in a railway line
returns to a railway company referred to in that section and, before the
day on which the return takes place, an agreement was in force between
the person or entity that owned the railway line or had the operating
interest in the railway line immediately before the return and a public
passenger service provider as defined in section 87 in respect of the
operation of a passenger rail service on that railway line, then, unless
the public passenger service provider indicates otherwise before that
day, the rights and obligations of the person or entity under the
agreement in respect of the operation of that service on that line vest,
as of that day, in the railway company and the railway company shall
resume operations of the railway line.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

- **[[146.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A railway company that discontinues
  operating a grain-dependent branch line listed in Schedule I, or a
  portion of one, that is in a municipality or district shall,
  commencing on the date on which notice was provided under subsection
  146(1), make three annual payments to the municipality or district in
  the amount equal to \$10,000 for each mile of the line or portion in
  the municipality or district.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a railway company to which subsection 146.01(1)
  applies does not resume operations on a grain-dependent branch line
  listed in Schedule I within the period provided for in that subsection
  and does not enter into an agreement for the sale, lease or other
  transfer of that railway line, or applicable interest in that railway
  line, after following the process set out in sections 143 to 145, the
  railway company shall, beginning on the day after the last day on
  which its offer could have been accepted under section 145, make the
  annual payments referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 16, s. 8
- 2007, c. 19, s. 41(F)
- 2008, c. 5, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146.1-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 146.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of metropolitan sidings and spurs to
be dismantled

- **[[146.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A railway company shall prepare and
  keep up to date a list of its sidings and spurs that it plans to
  dismantle and that are located in metropolitan areas or within the
  territory served by any urban transit authority, except for sidings
  and spurs located on a railway right-of-way that will continue to be
  used for railway operations subsequent to their dismantlement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of list and notification of
  changes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall publish the list on its
  Internet site and, whenever it makes a change to the list, it shall
  notify the following of the change within 10 days after the change:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Agency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the minister responsible for transportation matters
    in the government of the province in which the siding or spur that
    is the subject of the change is located;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the chairperson of the urban transit authority in
    whose territory the siding or spur that is the subject of the change
    is located; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the clerk or other senior administrative officer of
    the municipal or district government in which the siding or spur
    that is the subject of the change is located.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A railway company shall not take steps to dismantle a
  siding or a spur until at least 12 months have elapsed since the
  siding or spur was added to the list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer to governments

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Before dismantling a siding or a spur that has been
  on the list for at least 12 months, a railway company shall send
  simultaneously to each of the following an offer to transfer all of
  its interest in the siding or spur for not more than its net salvage
  value:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the minister responsible for transportation matters
    in the government of the province in which the siding or spur is
    located;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the chairperson of the urban transit authority in
    whose territory the siding or spur is located; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the clerk or other senior administrative officer of
    the municipal or district government in which the siding or spur is
    located.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits for acceptance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 146.3(3), after the offer is
  received

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by the Minister, the Government of Canada may
    accept it within 30 days;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the provincial minister, the government of the
    province may accept it within an additional 30 days after the end of
    the period mentioned in paragraph (a) if it is not accepted under
    that paragraph;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by the chairperson of an urban transit authority,
    that authority may accept it within an additional 30 days after the
    end of the periods for acceptance under paragraphs (a) and (b), if
    it is not accepted under those paragraphs; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} by the clerk or other senior administrative officer
    of a municipal or district government, that government may accept it
    within an additional 30 days after the end of the periods for
    acceptance under paragraphs (a), (b) and (c), if it is not accepted
    under those paragraphs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication and notice of acceptance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Once a government or an urban transit authority
  communicates its written acceptance of the offer to the railway
  company, the right of any other government or urban transit authority
  to accept the offer is extinguished, and the railway company shall
  notify the other governments and urban transit authorities of the
  acceptance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Net salvage value

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a government or an urban transit authority accepts
  the offer, but cannot agree with the railway company on the net
  salvage value within 90 days after the acceptance, the Agency may, on
  the application of the government, the urban transit authority or the
  railway company, determine the net salvage value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismantling permitted

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the offer is not accepted, the railway company may
  dismantle the siding or spur on providing notice to the Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of net salvage value before
expiry of time to accept offer

- **[[146.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a railway line is
  offered under section 145, or to whom a siding or spur is offered
  under section 146.2, may apply to the Agency for a determination of
  the net salvage value of the railway line, siding or spur, as the case
  may be, at any time before the expiry of the period available to the
  person to accept the offer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The applicant shall without delay provide a copy of
  the application to the railway company, and the railway company shall
  without delay notify every other person to whom the offer was made and
  whose time to accept the offer has not expired that an application for
  a determination of the net salvage value was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an application is made under subsection (1), the
  time available to the applicant to accept the offer expires on the day
  that is 30 days after the day the Agency notifies the applicant of its
  determination of the net salvage value and the 30-day period for each
  other person to accept the offer is calculated on the expiry of the
  period available to the applicant to accept the offer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The applicant shall reimburse the Agency's costs
  associated with the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Railway rights of way

**[[146.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.4 .sectionLabel}** Sections 146.2 and
146.3 apply, with any modifications that are necessary, to railway
rights-of-way, that are located in metropolitan areas or within the
territory served by any urban transit authority and in respect of which
the sidings and spurs have been dismantled, that a railway company plans
to sell, lease or otherwise transfer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger railway stations

**[[146.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.5 .sectionLabel}** Sections 146.2 and
146.3 apply, with any modifications that are necessary, to passenger
railway stations in Canada that a railway company plans to sell, lease
or otherwise transfer or dismantle.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints

**[[146.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146.6 .sectionLabel}** If, after receiving
a complaint, the Agency finds that a railway company is not complying
with this Division, the Agency may order it to take any measures that
the Agency considers appropriate to comply with this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 38
:::

### [DIVISION VI]{.HLabel2}[Transportation of Western Grain]{.HTitleText2} {#h-56086 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56087 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[crop year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[crop year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period beginning on
    August 1 in any year and ending on July 31 in the next
    year; ([campagne agricole]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[export]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[export]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of grain, means shipment
    by a vessel, as defined in section 2 of the [Canada Shipping Act,
    2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15) , to any destination outside Canada and
    shipment by any other mode of transport to the United States for use
    of the grain in that country and not for shipment out of that
    country; ([exportation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[government hopper car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[government hopper car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2018, c.
    10, s. 39\]]{.Repealed}

[[grain]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[grain]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any grain or crop included in Schedule II that is
      grown in the Western Division, or any product of it included in
      Schedule II that is processed in the Western Division, or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any grain or crop included in Schedule II that is
      grown outside Canada and imported into Canada, or any product of
      any grain or crop included in Schedule II that is itself included
      in Schedule II and is processed outside Canada and imported into
      Canada; ([grain]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[joint line movement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[joint line movement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2000, c.
    16, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

[[maximum rate scale]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[maximum rate scale]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2000, c.
    16, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

[[movement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[movement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of grain, means the
    carriage of grain by a prescribed railway company over a railway
    line from a point on any line west of Thunder Bay or Armstrong,
    Ontario, to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Thunder Bay or Armstrong, Ontario,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Churchill, Manitoba for export,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a port in British Columbia for export, other than
      export to the United States for consumption in that country, or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a point west of Thunder Bay or Armstrong,
      Ontario, if the grain is to be carried to a port in British
      Columbia for export, other than export to the United States for
      consumption in that country; ([mouvement du
      grain]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[port in British Columbia]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[port in British Columbia]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes Vancouver,
    North Vancouver, New Westminster, Roberts Bank, Prince Rupert,
    Ridley Island, Burnaby, Fraser Mills, Fraser Surrey, Fraser Wharves,
    Lake City, Lulu Island Junction, Port Coquitlam, Port Moody,
    Steveston, Tilbury and Woodwards Landing; ([port de la
    Colombie-Britannique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[prescribed railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[prescribed railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    Canadian National Railway Company, the Canadian Pacific Railway
    Company and any railway company that may be specified in the
    regulations; ([compagnie de chemin de fer régie]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Western Division]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Western Division]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the part of Canada
    lying west of the meridian passing through the eastern boundary of
    the City of Thunder Bay, including the whole of the Province of
    Manitoba. ([région de l'Ouest]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 147
- 2000, c. 16, s. 9
- 2001, c. 26, s. 282
- 2005, c. 24, s. 3
- 2011, c. 25, s. 60
- 2018, c. 10, s. 39
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147-20150730.html "Link to previous version of section 147"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application of Division IV]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56108 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to tariffs and rates

**[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** The provisions of
Division IV apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require,
to tariffs and rates under this Division to the extent that those
provisions are not inconsistent with this Division.

#### [Tariffs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56111 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance and publication

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  prescribed railway company shall issue and publish tariffs that
  include single car rates in respect of the movement of grain from each
  grain delivery point on its railway.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No prescribed railway company shall establish a
  single car rate in a tariff in respect of the movement of grain from a
  grain delivery point on one of its branch lines that is more than
  three per cent higher than any single car rate in its tariffs for the
  movement of the same type of grain under substantially similar
  conditions for a substantially similar distance from the grain
  delivery point on one of its main lines that is nearest, as measured
  in a straight line, to the grain delivery point on the branch line.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 149, c. 18, s. 41
- 2000, c. 16, s. 10
:::

#### [Maximum Grain Revenue Entitlement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56119 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceiling

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  prescribed railway company's revenues, as determined by the Agency,
  for the movement of grain in a crop year may not exceed the company's
  maximum revenue entitlement for that year as determined under
  subsection 151(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of excess and penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a prescribed railway company's revenues, as
  determined by the Agency, for the movement of grain in a crop year
  exceed the company's maximum revenue entitlement for that year as
  determined under subsection 151(1), the company shall pay out the
  excess amount, and any penalty that may be specified in the
  regulations, in accordance with the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Items not included in revenue

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a prescribed
  railway company's revenue for the movement of grain in a crop year
  shall not include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} incentives, rebates or any similar reductions paid
    or allowed by the company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any amount that is earned by the company and that
    the Agency determines is reasonable to characterize as a performance
    penalty or as being in respect of demurrage or for the storage of
    railway cars loaded with grain;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} compensation for running rights;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any amount that is earned by the company at the
    interswitching rate determined in accordance with section 127.1; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any amount that is earned by the company for the
    movement of grain in containers on flat cars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Impermissible reductions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a prescribed
  railway company's revenue for the movement of grain in a crop year
  shall not be reduced by amounts paid or allowed as dispatch by the
  company for loading or unloading grain before the expiry of the period
  agreed on for loading or unloading the grain.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reductions from revenue

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, if the Agency
  determines that it was reasonable for a prescribed railway company to
  make a contribution for the development of grain-related facilities to
  a grain handling undertaking that is not owned by the company, the
  company's revenue for the movement of grain in a crop year shall be
  reduced by any amount that the Agency determines constitutes the
  amortized amount of the contribution by the company in the crop year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency to determine revenue

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall make the determination of a
  prescribed railway company's revenues for the movement of grain in a
  crop year on or before December 31 of the following crop year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 150
- 2000, c. 16, s. 10
- 2018, c. 10, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-150-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 150"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[150.1 to 150.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150.1_to_150.4
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2000, c. 16, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum revenue entitlement

- **[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  prescribed railway company's maximum revenue entitlement for the
  movement of grain in a crop year is the amount determined by the
  Agency in accordance with the formula

  ::: {#56144 .Subsection}
  \[A/B + ((C - D) × \$0.022)\] × E × F

  where

  [A]{.dfn}
  :   is the company's revenues for the movement of grain in the base
      year;

  [B]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of tonnes of grain involved in the company's
      movement of grain in the base year;

  [C]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of miles of the company's average length of haul for
      the movement of grain in that crop year as determined by the
      Agency;

  [D]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of miles of the company's average length of haul for
      the movement of grain in the base year;

  [E]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of tonnes of grain involved in the company's
      movement of grain in the crop year as determined by the Agency;
      and

  [F]{.dfn}
  :   is the volume-related composite price index that applies to the
      company, as determined by the Agency.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian National Railway Company

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), in the case of
  the Canadian National Railway Company,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} A is \$348,000,000;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} B is 12,437,000; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} D is 1,045.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian Pacific Railway Company

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), in the case of
  the Canadian Pacific Railway Company,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} A is \$362,900,000;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} B is 13,894,000; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} D is 897.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Volume-related composite price index

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The following rules are applicable to a
  volume-related composite price index:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the crop year 2016-2017, each prescribed railway
    company's index is 1.3275;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an index shall be determined in respect of each
    prescribed railway company; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Agency shall make adjustments to each
    prescribed railway company's index to reflect the costs incurred by
    the prescribed railway company to obtain hopper cars for the
    movement of grain and the costs incurred by the prescribed railway
    company for the maintenance of those hopper cars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When Agency to make determination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall make the determination of a
  prescribed railway company's maximum revenue entitlement for the
  movement of grain in a crop year under subsection (1) on or before
  December 31 of the following crop year and shall make the
  determination of a prescribed railway company's volume-related
  composite price index on or before April 30 of the previous crop year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Making of adjustments

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (5), the Agency shall make the
  adjustments referred to in paragraph (4)(c) at any time that it
  considers appropriate and determine the date when the adjusted index
  takes effect.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 151
- 2000, c. 16, s. 10
- 2007, c. 19, s. 43
- 2018, c. 10, s. 41
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-151-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 151"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Minister

- **[[151.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before the beginning of every crop
  year, a prescribed railway company shall provide to the Minister a
  report, in the form and manner that may be specified by the Minister,
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assesses the prescribed railway company's ability
    to move the grain that it is required to move during the crop year
    taking into account the total volume of grain expected to be moved
    for the crop year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identifies the steps that the prescribed railway
    company is taking to enable it to move the grain that it is required
    to move during the crop year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report --- winter contingency plans

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before October 1 of every year, a prescribed railway
  company shall provide to the Minister a report, in the form and manner
  that may be specified by the Minister, that describes the railway
  company's contingency plans to enable it to move the grain along with
  other traffic when faced with winter weather conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed railway company shall publish the
  reports referred to in subsections (1) and (2) on its Internet site at
  the same time that they are provided to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 10, s. 42
:::

#### [List of Available Sidings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56184 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of available sidings

- **[[151.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A prescribed railway company shall
  prepare and keep up to date a list of the sidings that it makes
  available in the Western Division where railway cars that are
  allocated by the Canadian Grain Commission under subsection 87(2) of
  the [Canada Grain Act](/eng/acts/G-10) can be loaded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of list

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The railway company shall publish the list on its
  Internet site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal from list

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The railway company may remove a siding from the list
  only after the expiry of 60 days after the publication of a notice of
  its intention to do so in a newspaper of general circulation in the
  area where the siding is located.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 6
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56194 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying railway companies for the purposes of the
  definition [[prescribed railway company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
  section 147;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifying, in respect of a prescribed railway
  company other than the Canadian National Railway Company or the
  Canadian Pacific Railway Company,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} revenues for the movement of grain in the base
    year,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the number of tonnes for the movement of grain in
    the base year, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the average length of haul for the movement of
    grain in the base year;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} specifying a penalty for the purposes of subsection
  150(2) and respecting the paying out of the penalty and the excess
  amount; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for carrying out the purposes and provisions of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 152
- 2000, c. 16, s. 10
:::

### [DIVISION VI.1]{.HLabel2}[Public Passenger Service Providers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-56206 .Subheading}

#### [Dispute Resolution]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56207 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[152.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Whenever a public passenger service
  provider and a railway company are unable to agree in respect of any
  matter raised in the context of the negotiation of any agreement
  concerning the use of the railway company's railway, land, equipment,
  facilities or services by the public passenger service provider or
  concerning the conditions, or the amount to be paid, for that use, the
  public passenger service provider may, after reasonable efforts to
  resolve the matter have been made, apply to the Agency to decide the
  matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Whenever a public passenger service provider and a
  railway company are unable to agree in respect of any matter raised in
  the context of the implementation of any matter previously decided by
  the Agency, either the public passenger service provider or the
  railway company may, after reasonable efforts to resolve the matter
  have been made, apply to the Agency to decide the matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 44
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount to be fixed

- **[[152.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, pursuant to an application made
  under subsection 152.1(1), the Agency fixes the amount to be paid by
  the public passenger service provider for the use of any of the
  railway company's railway, land, equipment, facilities or services,
  that amount must reflect the cost associated with the public passenger
  service provider's use of that railway, land or equipment or those
  facilities or services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining that amount, the Agency must take into
  consideration, among other things,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the variable costs incurred by the railway company
    as a result of the public passenger service provider's use of the
    railway company's railway, land, equipment, facilities or services,
    including, but not limited to, its variable costs incurred to
    maintain safe operations and to avoid congestion and undue delay;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the railway company's cost of capital, based on a
    rate set by the Agency, applied to the net book value of the assets
    to be used by the public passenger service provider, less any amount
    to be paid by the public passenger service provider in respect of
    those assets;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the cost of any improvements made by the railway
    company in relation to the public passenger service provider's use
    of the railway company's railway, land, equipment, facilities or
    services;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a reasonable contribution towards the railway
    company's constant costs; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the value of any benefits that would accrue to the
    railway company from any investment made by the public passenger
    service provider.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 44
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of decision

**[[152.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152.3 .sectionLabel}** Any decision of the
Agency in respect of an application made under subsection 152.1(1) is
binding on the parties for a period of five years after the day on which
the decision is made, or for any other period agreed to by the parties
that is specified in the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 44
:::

#### [Agreements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-56231 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Providing copies

- **[[152.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A railway company or a public
  passenger service provider must provide to any person who requests it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of any agreement entered into on or after
    the day on which this section comes into force concerning the use of
    the railway company's railway, land, equipment, facilities or
    services; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), a copy of any agreement
    entered into before the day on which this section comes into force
    concerning the use of the railway company's railway, land,
    equipment, facilities or services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, on application by a railway company
