  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The shipper and the carrier shall share equally,
  whether or not the proceedings are terminated pursuant to section 168,
  in the payment of the fee fixed under subsection (1) and in the cost

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} borne by the Agency for administrative, technical
    and legal services provided to the arbitrator pursuant to subsection
    162(2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} of the preparation of any reasons requested
    pursuant to subsection 165(5).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of information

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** Where the Agency is
advised that a party to a final offer arbitration wishes to keep matters
relating to the arbitration confidential,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Agency and the arbitrator shall take all
  reasonably necessary measures to ensure that the matters are not
  disclosed by the Agency or the arbitrator or during the arbitration
  proceedings to any person other than the parties; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no reasons for the decision given pursuant to
  subsection 165(5) shall contain those matters or any information
  included in a contract that the parties agreed to keep confidential.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of proceedings

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** Where, before the
arbitrator renders a decision on a final offer arbitration, the parties
advise the Agency or the arbitrator that they agree that the matter
being arbitrated should be withdrawn from arbitration, the arbitration
proceedings in respect of the matter shall be immediately terminated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of arbitrators

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency shall, from time to time, in consultation with representatives
  of shippers and carriers, establish a list of persons who agree to act
  as arbitrators in final offer arbitrations. The list must state which
  of the persons have indicated that they have expertise that may assist
  them in conducting final offer arbitrations and the nature of that
  expertise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List per mode

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A separate list of persons may be established under
  subsection (1) in respect of each or any mode of transportation, as
  the Agency considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of list

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall have the list of persons made known
  to representatives of shippers and carriers throughout Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 169
- 2000, c. 16, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediation

- **[[169.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The parties to a final offer
  arbitration may, by agreement, refer to a mediator, which may be the
  Agency, a matter that has been submitted for a final offer arbitration
  under section 161.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of roster

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may establish a roster of persons, which
  may include members and staff of the Agency, to act as mediators in
  any matter referred to it under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of mediation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All matters relating to the mediation shall be kept
  confidential, unless the parties otherwise agree, and information
  provided by a party for the purposes of the mediation shall not be
  used for any other purpose without the consent of that party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for completion of mediation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless the parties otherwise agree, the mediation
  shall be completed within 30 days after the matter is referred for
  mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of mediation on final offer
  arbitration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The mediation has the effect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} staying the conduct of the final offer arbitration
    for the period of the mediation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} extending the time within which the arbitrator must
    make a decision in the matter of the final offer arbitration by the
    period of the mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediator not to act in other
  proceedings

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The person who acts as mediator may not act in any
  other proceedings in relation to any matter that was at issue in the
  mediation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint offer of several shippers

- **[[169.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the case where more than one
  shipper is dissatisfied with the rate or rates charged or proposed to
  be charged by a carrier for the movement of goods, or with any
  conditions associated with the movement of goods, those shippers may,
  if the matter cannot be resolved between them and the carrier, submit
  the matter jointly to the Agency for a final offer arbitration, in
  which case sections 161 to 169 apply, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common matter and application of the
  offer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A matter submitted jointly to the Agency for a final
  offer arbitration shall be common to all the shippers and the shippers
  shall make a joint offer in respect of the matter, the terms of which
  apply to all of them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration precluded in certain cases

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall not have any matter submitted to it
  for a final offer arbitration under subsection (1) arbitrated unless
  the shippers demonstrate, to the satisfaction of the Agency, that an
  attempt has been made to mediate the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of mediation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All matters relating to a mediation shall be kept
  confidential, unless the parties otherwise agree, and information
  provided by a party for the purposes of the mediation shall not be
  used for any other purpose without the consent of that party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediator not to act in other
  proceedings

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The person who acts as mediator may not act in any
  other proceedings in relation to any matter that was at issue in the
  mediation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matter submitted by more than one
  shipper

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a matter that is submitted jointly
  under subsection (1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period referred to in subsection 161.1(1) is 20
    days;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the arbitrator may, if he or she considers it
    necessary, extend any of the periods referred to in subsections
    163(3) and (4) and paragraph 164.1(a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the decision of the arbitrator shall, despite
    paragraph 165(2)(b), be rendered within 120 days or, in the case of
    an arbitration conducted in accordance with section 164.1, 90 days
    after the day on which the submission for the final offer
    arbitration is received by the Agency unless the parties agree
    otherwise.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- preliminary applications

- **[[169.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite sections 162 and 162.1, any
  application filed with the Agency by a carrier in respect of a matter
  submitted jointly to the Agency under subsection 169.2(1) shall be
  filed with the Agency no later than seven days after the day on which
  the joint submission is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of copy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the application shall be served on each of
  the shippers making the joint submission no later than the day on
  which the application is required to be filed under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint answer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The shippers, no later than five days after the day
  on which the last shipper was served under subsection (2), shall file
  with the Agency a joint answer to the application and serve a copy of
  it on the carrier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The carrier, no later than two days after the day on
  which it was served under subsection (3), shall file with the Agency a
  reply to the joint answer and serve a copy of it on each of the
  shippers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of Agency

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Agency shall issue its decision on the
  application no later than the day on which the matter is required to
  be referred to arbitration under subsection 162(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed conformity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If no application referred to in subsection (1) is
  filed within the limit set out in that subsection, the matter
  submitted jointly is deemed to conform to the requirements of
  subsection 169.2(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 5, s. 7
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Arbitration on Level of Services]{.HTitleText2} {#h-56934 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission for arbitration ---
confidential contract

- **[[169.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a shipper and a railway company
  are unable to agree and enter into a contract under subsection 126(1)
  respecting the manner in which the railway company must fulfil its
  service obligations under section 113, the shipper may submit any of
  the following matters, in writing, to the Agency for arbitration:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operational terms that the railway company must
    comply with in respect of receiving, loading, carrying, unloading
    and delivering the traffic, including performance standards and
    communication protocols;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operational terms that the railway company must
    comply with if it fails to comply with an operational term described
    in paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any operational term that the shipper must comply
    with that is related to an operational term described in
    paragraph (a) or (b);

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} any amount to be paid by the company or the
    shipper in relation to a failure to comply with any operational term
    described in paragraphs (a) to (c);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any service provided by the railway company
    incidental to transportation that is customary or usual in
    connection with the business of a railway company;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the question of whether the railway company may
    apply a charge with respect to an operational term described in
    paragraph (a) or (b) or for a service described in paragraph (d); or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the dispute resolution process related to the
    implementation of the arbitrator's decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may make regulations specifying what
  constitutes operational terms for the purposes of paragraphs (1)(a) to
  (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matter excluded from arbitration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The shipper is not entitled to submit to the Agency
  for arbitration a matter that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is governed by a written agreement, including a
    confidential contract, to which the shipper and the railway company
    are parties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of an order, other than an interim
    order, made under subsection 116(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded matter --- traffic

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The shipper is not entitled to submit to the Agency
  for arbitration a matter that is in respect of traffic that is the
  subject of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a confidential contract between the shipper and the
    railway company that is in force immediately before the day on which
    this section comes into force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a tariff, or a contract, referred to in subsection
    165(3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a long-haul interswitching order made under
    subsection 134(1); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an arbitrator's decision made under section 169.37.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a rate for the movement of the
  traffic is not to be subject to arbitration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
- 2014, c. 8, s. 8
- 2018, c. 10, ss. 50, 95
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169.31-20170801.html "Link to previous version of section 169.31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of submission

- **[[169.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The submission must contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a detailed description of the matters submitted to
    the Agency for arbitration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the traffic to which the service
    obligations relate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an undertaking with respect to the traffic, if any,
    given by the shipper to the railway company that must be complied
    with for the period during which the arbitrator's decision applies
    to the parties, other than an undertaking given by the shipper to
    the railway company with respect to an operational term described in
    paragraph 169.31(1)(c);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an undertaking given by the shipper to the railway
    company to ship the goods to which the service obligations relate in
    accordance with the arbitrator's decision; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an undertaking given by the shipper to the Agency
    to pay the fee and costs for which the shipper is liable under
    subsection 169.39(3) as a party to the arbitration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of submission served

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The shipper must serve a copy of the submission on
  the railway company on the day on which it submits the matters to the
  Agency for arbitration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration precluded in certain cases

- **[[169.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency must dismiss the
  submission if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the shipper has not, at least 15 days before making
    it, served on the railway company and the Agency a written notice
    indicating that the shipper intends to make a submission to the
    Agency for arbitration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the shipper does not demonstrate, to the Agency's
    satisfaction, that an attempt has been made to resolve the matters
    contained in it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice must contain the descriptions referred to
  in paragraphs 169.32(1)(a) and (b) and, if the shipper's submission
  will contain an undertaking described in paragraph 169.32(1)(c), a
  description of that undertaking.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of proposals

- **[[169.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite any application filed under
  section 169.43, the shipper and the railway company must each submit,
  within 10 days after the day on which a copy of a submission is served
  under subsection 169.32(2), to the Agency, in order to resolve the
  matters that are submitted to it for arbitration by the shipper, a
  proposal that contains any of the following terms:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any operational term described in paragraph
    169.31(1)(a), (b) or (c);

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to an amount described in
    paragraph 169.31(1)(c.1), if the matter in respect of the amount has
    been submitted by the shipper for arbitration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any term for the provision of a service described
    in paragraph 169.31(1)(d);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to the application of a
    charge described in paragraph 169.31(1)(e); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to the dispute resolution
    process described in paragraph 169.31(1)(f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposals provided to parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Agency must provide the shipper and the railway
  company with a copy of the other party's proposal immediately after
  the day on which it receives the last of the two proposals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exchange of information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The parties must exchange the information that they
  intend to submit to the arbitrator in support of their proposals
  within 20 days after the day on which a copy of a submission is served
  under subsection 169.32(2) or within a period agreed to by the parties
  or fixed by the arbitrator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless the parties agree otherwise, a party to the
  arbitration is not, in support of the proposal it submits under
  subsection (1), to refer to any offer, or any part of an offer, that
  was made to it --- before a copy of the submission is served under
  subsection 169.32(2) --- by the other party to the arbitration for the
  purpose of entering into a confidential contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If no proposal from party

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If one party does not submit a proposal in accordance
  with subsection (1), the proposal submitted by the other party is the
  arbitrator's decision made under section 169.37.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
- 2018, c. 10, s. 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169.34-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 169.34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration

- **[[169.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite any application filed under
  section 169.43, the Agency must refer, within two business days after
  the day on which it receives the last of the two proposals, the
  matters for arbitration to be conducted by an arbitrator that it
  chooses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitrator not to act in other
  proceedings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator is not to act in any other proceedings
  in relation to a matter that is referred to him or her for
  arbitration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance by Agency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, at the arbitrator's request, provide
  administrative, technical and legal assistance to the arbitrator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration not proceeding

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The arbitration is not a proceeding before the
  Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agency's rules of procedure

- **[[169.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may make rules of
  procedure for an arbitration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure generally

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any rule of procedure made by the Agency
  and in the absence of an agreement between the arbitrator and the
  parties as to the procedure to be followed, the arbitrator must
  conduct the arbitration as quickly as possible and in the manner that
  he or she considers appropriate having regard to the circumstances of
  the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Questions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each party may direct questions to the other in the
  manner that the arbitrator considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitrator's decision

- **[[169.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator's decision must
  establish the following terms, or any combination of the following
  terms, that the arbitrator considers necessary to resolve the matters
  that are referred to him or her for arbitration:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any operational term described in paragraph
    169.31(1)(a), (b) or (c);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to an amount described in
    paragraph 169.31(1)(c.1) if the matter in respect of the amount has
    been submitted by the shipper for arbitration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any term for the provision of a service described
    in paragraph 169.31(1)(d);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to the application of a
    charge described in paragraph 169.31(1)(e); or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any term with respect to the dispute resolution
    process described in paragraph 169.31(1)(f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Elements to consider

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In making the decision, the arbitrator must have
  regard to the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the traffic to which the service obligations
    relate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the service that the shipper requires with respect
    to the traffic;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any undertaking described in paragraph 169.32(1)(c)
    that is contained in the shipper's submission;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the railway company's obligations under this Act in
    respect of the operation of the railway;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the railway company's obligations, if any, with
    respect to a public passenger service provider;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the railway company's and the shipper's operational
    requirements and restrictions;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the question of whether there is available to the
    shipper an alternative, effective, adequate and competitive means of
    transporting the goods to which the service obligations relate; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any information that the arbitrator considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Efficiency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator shall establish a term with respect to
  an amount described in paragraph 169.31(1)(c.1) in a manner that
  encourages the efficient movement of the shipper's traffic and the
  performance of the railway system and that is balanced between the
  shipper and the railway company.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
- 2018, c. 10, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169.37-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 169.37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements of decision

- **[[169.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator's decision must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be made in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be made so as to apply to the parties for a period
    of one year as of the date of his or her decision, unless the
    parties agree otherwise; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be commercially fair and reasonable to the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision binding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator's decision is final and binding on the
  parties and is deemed, for the purposes of Division IV of Part III and
  its enforceability between the parties, to be a confidential contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for making decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator's decision must be made within 45 days
  after the day on which the matters are submitted to the Agency for
  arbitration under subsection 169.31(1) unless in the arbitrator's
  opinion making a decision within that period is not practical, in
  which case the arbitrator must make his or her decision within 65 days
  after that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period --- agreement of parties

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), the arbitrator may, with the
  agreement of the parties, make his or her decision within a period
  that is longer than 65 days after the day on which the matters are
  submitted to the Agency for arbitration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of decision to Agency

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The arbitrator must provide the Agency with a copy of
  his or her decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration fees

- **[[169.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may fix the fee to be
  paid to it or, if the arbitrator is not a member or on the staff of
  the Agency, to the arbitrator for the arbitrator's services in
  arbitration proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration fees --- not member

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An arbitrator who is not a member or on the staff of
  the Agency may fix a fee for his or her services if the Agency does
  not do so under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of fees and costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The shipper and the railway company are to share
  equally, whether or not the proceedings are terminated under section
  169.41, in the payment of the fee for the arbitrator's services and in
  the payment of the costs related to the arbitration, including those
  borne by the Agency in providing administrative, technical and legal
  assistance to the arbitrator under subsection 169.35(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cost related to arbitration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Costs related to the arbitration also include the
  cost to the Agency when a member or a person on the staff of the
  Agency acts as an arbitrator and the Agency does not fix a fee for
  that arbitrator under subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of information

- **[[169.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency and the arbitrator are
  advised that a party to an arbitration wishes to keep information
  relating to the arbitration confidential, the Agency and the
  arbitrator must take all reasonably necessary measures to ensure that
  the information is not disclosed by the Agency or the arbitrator or
  during the arbitration to any person other than the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited disclosure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Agency may, in the
  exercise of its powers or in the performance of its duties and
  functions under this Act, disclose any information that a party
  advised the Agency and the arbitrator it wishes to keep confidential.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of proceedings

**[[169.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.41 .sectionLabel}** If, before the
arbitrator makes his or her decision, the parties advise the Agency or
the arbitrator that they agree that the matters being arbitrated should
be withdrawn from arbitration, any proceedings in respect of those
matters are immediately terminated.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of arbitrators

- **[[169.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency, in consultation with
  representatives of shippers and railway companies, must establish a
  list of persons, including persons who are members or on the staff of
  the Agency, who agree to act as arbitrators in arbitrations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expertise required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Only persons who, in the Agency's opinion, have
  sufficient expertise to act as arbitrators are to be named in the
  list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of list

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency must publish the list on its Internet
  site.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for order

- **[[169.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A railway company may apply to the
  Agency, within 10 days after the day on which it is served with a copy
  of a submission under subsection 169.32(2), for an order declaring
  that the shipper is not entitled to submit to the Agency for
  arbitration a matter contained in the shipper's submission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency makes the order, it may also

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the submission for arbitration, if the
    matter contained in it has not been referred to arbitration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discontinue the arbitration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject the arbitration to any terms that it
    specifies; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} set aside the arbitrator's decision or any part of
    it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for making decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency must make a decision on the railway
  company's application made under subsection (1) as soon as feasible
  but not later than 35 days after the day on which it receives the
  application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 31, s. 11
:::

## [PART V]{.HLabel1}[Transportation of Persons with Disabilities]{.HTitleText1} {#h-57102 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[169.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.5 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in paragraph 5(d.1) and this Part.

[[barrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[barrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means anything --- including
    anything physical, architectural, technological or attitudinal,
    anything that is based on information or communications or anything
    that is the result of a policy or a practice --- that hinders the
    full and equal participation in society of persons with an
    impairment, including a physical, mental, intellectual, cognitive,
    learning, communication or sensory impairment or a functional
    limitation. ([obstacle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[disability]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[disability]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any impairment, including a
    physical, mental, intellectual, cognitive, learning, communication
    or sensory impairment --- or a functional limitation --- whether
    permanent, temporary or episodic in nature, or evident or not, that,
    in interaction with a barrier, hinders a person's full and equal
    participation in society. ([handicap]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 169](#1154743-1191629){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154743-1191629.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may, after consulting with the Minister, make regulations for
  the purpose of identifying or removing barriers or preventing new
  barriers --- particularly barriers in the built environment,
  information and communication technologies and the design and delivery
  of programs and services --- in the transportation network under the
  legislative authority of Parliament to the mobility of persons with
  disabilities, including regulations respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the design, construction or modification of, and
    the posting of signs on, in or around, means of transportation and
    related facilities and premises, including equipment used in them;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the training of personnel employed at or in those
    facilities or premises or by carriers;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} tariffs, rates, fares, charges and terms and
    conditions of carriage applicable in respect of the transportation
    of persons with disabilities or incidental services; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the communication of information to persons with
    disabilities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) incorporating
  standards or enactments by reference may incorporate them as amended
  from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, with the approval of the Governor in
  Council, make orders exempting specified persons, means of
  transportation, services or related facilities and premises from the
  application of regulations made under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On application and after consulting with the
  Minister, the Agency may, by order, on any terms that it considers
  necessary,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exempt a person from the application of regulations
    made under subsection (1) if the Agency is satisfied that the person
    has taken or will take measures --- at least equivalent to those
    that must be taken under the provisions of the regulations for which
    an exemption is to be granted --- to remove barriers or to prevent
    new barriers; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempt a class of persons from the application of
    regulations made under subsection (1) if the Agency is satisfied
    that all the members of the class have taken or will take measures
    --- at least equivalent to those that must be taken under the
    provisions of the regulations for which an exemption is to be
    granted --- to remove barriers or to prevent new barriers.

  The order ceases to have effect on the earlier of the end of the
  period of three years that begins on the day on which the order is
  made and the end of any shorter period specified in the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 170
- [2019, c. 10, s. 170](#1154745-1191638){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154745-1191638.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coordination

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** The Agency and the
Canadian Human Rights Commission shall coordinate their activities in
relation to the transportation of persons with disabilities in order to
foster complementary policies and practices and to avoid jurisdictional
conflicts.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 171
- [2019, c. 10, s. 171(F)](#1154752-1191641){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154752-1191641.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-171-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 171"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry --- barriers to mobility

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may, on application, inquire into a matter in relation to which
  a regulation could be made under subsection 170(1), regardless of
  whether such a regulation has been made, in order to determine whether
  there is an undue barrier to the mobility of persons with
  disabilities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On determining that there is an undue barrier to the
  mobility of persons with disabilities, the Agency may do one or more
  of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require the taking of appropriate corrective
    measures;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid for any expense
    incurred by a person with a disability arising out of the barrier,
    including for any costs of obtaining alternative goods, services or
    accommodation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid for any wages that
    a person with a disability was deprived of as a result of the
    barrier;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid up to a maximum
    amount of --- subject to the annual adjustments made under section
    172.2 --- \$20,000, for any pain and suffering experienced by a
    person with a disability arising out of the barrier;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid up to a maximum
    amount of --- subject to the annual adjustments made under section
    172.2 --- \$20,000, if the Agency determines that the barrier is the
    result of a wilful or reckless practice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency is satisfied that regulations made
  under subsection 170(1) that are applicable in relation to a matter
  have been complied with or have not been contravened, the Agency may
  determine that there is an undue barrier in relation to that matter
  but if it does so, it may only require the taking of appropriate
  corrective measures.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 172
- [2019, c. 10, s. 172](#1154754-1191656){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154754-1191656.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 172"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry --- subsection 170(1)

- **[[172.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Agency may, on application,
  inquire into a matter concerning any regulations made under subsection
  170(1) to determine if the applicant has suffered physical or
  psychological harm, property damage or economic loss arising out of
  --- or has otherwise been adversely affected by --- a contravention of
  any provision of those regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On determining that an applicant has suffered
  physical or psychological harm, property damage or economic loss
  arising out of --- or has otherwise been adversely affected by --- a
  contravention referred to in subsection (1), the Agency may do one or
  more of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require the taking of appropriate corrective
    measures;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid to the applicant
    for any expense incurred by them arising out of the contravention,
    including for any costs of obtaining alternative goods, services or
    accommodation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid to the applicant
    for any wages that they were deprived of as a result of the
    contravention;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid to the applicant
    up to a maximum amount of --- subject to the annual adjustments made
    under section 172.2 --- \$20,000, for any pain and suffering
    experienced by them arising out of the contravention;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} direct that compensation be paid to the applicant
    up to a maximum amount of --- subject to the annual adjustments made
    under section 172.2 --- \$20,000, if the Agency determines that the
    contravention is the result of a wilful or reckless practice.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 172](#1154754-1191668){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154754-1191668.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual adjustment

- **[[172.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraphs
  172(2)(d) and (e) and 172.1(2)(d) and (e), the maximum amount shall be
  adjusted annually so that in any calendar year following the one
  during which this section comes into force it is equivalent to the
  product of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount that would have been the maximum amount
    for that following calendar year if no adjustment had been made
    under this section with respect to that year, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ratio that the Consumer Price Index for the
    preceding calendar year bears to the Consumer Price Index for the
    calendar year next before that preceding calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consumer Price Index

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a reference to the
  Consumer Price Index for any 12-month period means the average of the
  Consumer Price Index for Canada, as published by Statistics Canada
  under the authority of the [Statistics Act](/eng/acts/S-19), for each
  month in that 12-month period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of adjusted amount

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a maximum amount is adjusted in accordance with
  this section, the Agency shall publish the maximum amount as so
  adjusted as soon as it is determined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A direction to pay compensation under any of
  paragraphs 172(2)(b) and (c) and 172.1(2)(b) and (c) may include an
  award of interest at a rate and for a period that the Agency considers
  appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 172](#1154754-1191681){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154754-1191681.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry --- transportation of persons
with disabilities

**[[172.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.3 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may on
its own initiative, with the Minister's approval and on any terms that
he or she considers appropriate, inquire into any matter or thing that
concerns transportation to which the legislative authority of Parliament
extends and that relates to the mobility of persons with disabilities.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 172](#1154754-1191685){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154754-1191685.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participant funding program

**[[172.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.4 .sectionLabel}** The Agency may
establish a participant funding program to facilitate the participation
of persons with disabilities in hearings that are held for the purposes
of inquiries made under section 172, 172.1 or 172.3.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 172](#1154754-1191689){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154754-1191689.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [PART VI]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-57123 .Part}

### [Enforcement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-57124 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information, etc.

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall knowingly make any false or misleading statement or
  knowingly provide false or misleading information to the Agency or the
  Minister or to any person acting on behalf of the Agency or the
  Minister in connection with any matter under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obstruction and false statements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall knowingly obstruct or hinder, or make
  any false or misleading statement, either orally or in writing, to a
  person designated as an enforcement officer pursuant to paragraph
  178(1)(a) who is engaged in carrying out functions under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes a provision of this Act or a regulation or order made under
this Act, other than an order made under section 47, is guilty of an
offence punishable on summary conviction and liable

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an individual, to a fine not exceeding
  \$5,000; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, to a fine not exceeding
  \$25,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, etc., of corporation re
offences

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** Where a corporation
commits an offence under this Act, every person who at the time of the
commission of the offence was a director or officer of the corporation
is guilty of the like offence unless the act or omission constituting
the offence took place without the person's knowledge or consent or the
person exercised all due diligence to prevent the commission of the
offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for commencement of
proceedings

**[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** Proceedings by way of
summary conviction in respect of an offence under this Act may be
instituted within but not later than twelve months after the time when
the subject-matter of the proceedings arose.

### [Administrative Monetary Penalties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-57138 .Subheading}

Definition of [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[176.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 180.1 to 180.7, [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
Transportation Appeal Tribunal of Canada established by subsection 2(1)
of the [Transportation Appeal Tribunal of Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-18.5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 48
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulation-making powers

- **[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency may, by regulation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designate

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any provision of this Act or of any regulation,
      order or direction made pursuant to this Act,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of any provision referred to in
      subparagraph (i), or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any condition of a licence issued under this
      Act,

    as a provision, requirement or condition the contravention of which
    may be proceeded with as a violation in accordance with sections 179
    and 180; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribe the maximum amount payable for each
    violation, but the amount shall not exceed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} \$5,000, in the case of an individual, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} \$25,000 --- or, in the case of a violation
      involving a contravention of any provision of a regulation made
      under subsection 86.11(1), \$250,000 --- in the case of a
      corporation.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 10, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations by Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by regulation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designate as a provision or requirement the
    contravention of which may be proceeded with as a violation in
    accordance with sections 179 and 180 any provision of section 51 or
    51.2 or any provision of any regulation made under section 50 or 51,
    or any requirement of section 51 or 51.2 or those regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribe the maximum amount payable for each
    violation, but the amount shall not exceed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} \$5,000, in the case of an individual, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} \$25,000, in the case of a corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain provisions

  [(2.001)]{.lawlabel} The contravention of any provision of a
  regulation made under section 47.1 or subsection 50(1.001) or of any
  provision of an order made under subsection 51.5(1) or the
  contravention of any of subsections 51(1), (3) and (4) and 51.11(1)
  and (2) may be proceeded with as a violation in accordance with
  sections 179 and 180. The maximum amount payable for each violation is
  \$100,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations made under subsection
  50(1.01)

  [(2.01)]{.lawlabel} The contravention of any provision of a regulation
  made under subsection 50(1.01) may be proceeded with as a violation in
  accordance with sections 179 and 180. The maximum amount payable for
  each violation is \$25,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sections 93.1 or 94

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The contravention of section 93.1 or 94 may be
  proceeded with as a violation in accordance with sections 179 and 180.
  The maximum amount payable for each violation is \$100,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsections 155.7(1) or 155.84(1) to
  (3)

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The contravention of subsection 155.7(1) or any of
  subsections 155.84(1) to (3) may be proceeded with as a violation in
  accordance with sections 179 and 180. The maximum amount payable for
  each violation is \$100,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 170(1) and [Accessible
  Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The contravention of any provision of any regulation
  made under subsection 170(1) --- or the contravention of any of
  subsections 60(1) to (4) and (7), 61(1) to (3) and 62(1) to (3)
  and (6) of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6) --- may be
  proceeded with as a violation in accordance with sections 179 and 180.
  The maximum amount payable for each violation is \$250,000.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notices of violation

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Agency, in respect of a violation referred to in subsection 177(1),
  (2.1) or (3), or the Minister, in respect of a violation referred to
  in subsection 177(2), (2.001), (2.01) or (2.2), may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designate persons, or classes of persons, as
    enforcement officers who are authorized to issue notices of
    violation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish the form and content of notices of
    violation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of enforcement officers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person designated as an enforcement officer
  pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) has the powers of entry and inspection
  referred to in paragraph 39(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification of designated persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person designated as an enforcement officer
  pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) shall receive an authorization in
  prescribed form attesting to the person's designation and shall, on
  demand, present the authorization to any person from whom the
  enforcement officer requests information in the course of the
  enforcement officer's duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of designated persons

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a violation
  referred to in section 177 has been committed, a person designated as
  an enforcement officer under paragraph (1)(a) may require any person
  to produce for examination or reproduction all or part of any document
  or electronically stored data that the enforcement officer believes on
  reasonable grounds contain any information relevant to the enforcement
  of this Act or any of sections 60 to 62 of the [Accessible Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance to enforcement officers

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any person from whom documents or data are requested
  under subsection (4) shall provide all reasonable assistance in their
  power to enable the enforcement officer making the request to carry
  out the enforcement officer's duties and shall furnish any information
  that the enforcement officer reasonably requires for the purposes of
  this Act or any of sections 60 to 62 of the [Accessible Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 178
- 2007, c. 19, s. 50

- 2015, c. 31, ss. 12, 38

- [2023, c. 26, s. 447](#1410623-1414091){.wb-lbx

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other powers of enforcement officers

- **[[178.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer who enters a
  place for a purpose related to verifying compliance or preventing
  non-compliance with any provision of this Act or of any regulation,
  order or direction made under this Act or with any of sections 60 to
  62 of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6) may, for that
  purpose,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} open and examine any receptacle or package found in
    the place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} examine anything found in the place;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} examine any record, report, electronic data or
    other document that is found in the place and make copies of it or
    take extracts from it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any computer system at the
    place to examine any electronic data referred to in paragraph (c);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} reproduce any document from any electronic data
    referred to in paragraph (c), or cause it to be reproduced, in the
    form of a printout or other output;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} take the record, report or other document referred
    to in paragraph (c) or the printout or other output referred to in
    paragraph (e) for examination or copying;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any copying equipment at
    the place to make copies of any document;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} take photographs and make recordings and sketches;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} order the owner or person having possession of any
    thing to which any provision of this Act or of any regulation, order
    or direction made under this Act or any of sections 60 to 62 of the
    [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6) apply that is found in that
    place to move it or, for any time that may be necessary, not to move
    it or to restrict its movement;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} order the owner or person having possession of any
    conveyance that is found in the place to stop the conveyance, to
    move it or, for any time that may be necessary, not to move it or to
    restrict its movement;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} order any person in that place to establish their
    identity to the enforcement officer's satisfaction; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} order a person in that place to start any activity
    that is necessary for the purpose of the inspection or to stop any
    activity that impedes the inspection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means of telecommunication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of the exercise, for the purpose
  referred to in subsection (1), of the power of entry conferred under
  subsection 178(2), an enforcement officer is considered to have
  entered a place when accessing it remotely by a means of
  telecommunication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- place not accessible to
  the public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer who, by a means of
  telecommunication, accesses remotely a place that is not accessible to
  the public must do so with the knowledge of the owner or person in
  charge of the place and must be remotely in the place for no longer
  than the period necessary for the purpose referred to in subsection
  (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying individual

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer may be accompanied by any
  other individual the officer believes is necessary to help them
  exercise their powers or perform their duties or functions under this
  section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entering private property

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer and any individual
  accompanying them may enter and pass through private property, other
  than a dwelling-house on that property, in order to gain entry to a
  place referred to in subsection (1). For greater certainty, they are
  not liable for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The owner or other person in charge of a place
  entered by an enforcement officer for the purpose referred to in
  subsection (1) and every individual found in the place must give the
  enforcement officer all reasonable assistance in the individual's
  power and provide the enforcement officer with any information that
  the enforcement officer may reasonably require.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 10, s. 175](#1154765-1191725){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 448](#1410625-1414095){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violations

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes a
  provision, requirement or condition designated under section 177
  commits a violation and is liable to a penalty fixed under that
  section or, in the case of a contravention referred to in subsection
  177(3), either a penalty fixed under that subsection or a warning
  under subparagraph 180(b)(i).

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 8, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How contraventions may be proceeded
  with

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where any act or omission can be proceeded with as a
  violation or as an offence, proceedings may be commenced in respect of
  that act or omission as a violation or as an offence, but proceeding
  with it as a violation precludes proceeding with it as an offence, and
  proceeding with it as an offence precludes proceeding with it as a
  violation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nature of violation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a violation is not an offence
  and, accordingly, section 126 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  does not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing violation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A violation that is committed or continued on more
  than one day constitutes a separate violation for each day on which it
  is committed or continued.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 179
- 2007, c. 19, s. 51(F)
- 2014, c. 8, s. 11
- [2019, c. 10, s. 176](#1154767-1191727){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154767-1191727.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 449](#1410630-1414098){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410630-1414098.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20190711.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of notice of violation

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** If a person designated
as an enforcement officer under paragraph 178(1)(a) believes that a
person has committed a violation, the enforcement officer may issue, and
shall serve on the person, a notice of violation that names the person,
identifies the violation and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a contravention other than one
  referred to in paragraph (b), sets out the penalty for the violation
  that the person is liable to pay;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a contravention of any provision of
  any regulation made under subsection 170(1) or a contravention of any
  of subsections 60(1) to (4) and (7), 61(1) to (3) and 62(1) to (3)
  and (6) of the [Accessible Canada Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6)

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} contains a warning, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} sets out the penalty for the violation that the
    person is liable to pay, and a lesser amount that may be paid in
    complete satisfaction of the amount of the penalty if paid within
    the time and in the manner set out in the notice; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} sets out the particulars concerning the time for
  paying and the manner of paying the penalty, if there is one.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 10, s. 180
- 2001, c. 29, s. 52
- 2007, c. 19, s. 52
- 2018, c. 10, s. 55
- [2019, c. 10, s. 177](#1154771-1191735){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154771-1191735.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-180-20180523.html "Link to previous version of section 180"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Options

- **[[180.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4), a
  person who has been served with a notice of violation must either pay
  the amount of the penalty specified in the notice or file with the
  Tribunal a written request for a review of the facts of the alleged
  contravention or of the amount of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 170(1) or [Accessible Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6) --- warning

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been served with a notice of
  violation that contains a warning may file with the Agency a written
  request for a review of the facts of the alleged contravention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 170(1) or Accessible Canada
  Act  --- penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been served with a notice of
  violation that identifies a violation referred to in subsection 177(3)
  and that sets out a penalty for the violation must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay the amount of the penalty --- or the lesser
    amount --- specified in the notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} file with the Tribunal a written request for a
    review of the facts of the alleged contravention or of the amount of
    the penalty; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} request, within the time and in the manner set out
    in the notice, to enter into a compliance agreement with the Agency
    for the purpose of ensuring the person's compliance with the
    provision of the regulations or of the [Accessible Canada
    Act](/eng/acts/A-0.6) to which the violation relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations made under subsection
  86.11(1) --- penalty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been served with a notice of
  violation that identifies any provision of a regulation made under
  subsection 86.11(1) that was contravened and that sets out a penalty
  for the violation must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay the amount of the penalty specified in the
    notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} file with the Tribunal a written request for a
    review of the facts of the alleged contravention or of the amount of
    the penalty; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} request, within the time and in the manner set out
    in the notice, to enter into a compliance agreement with the Agency
    for the purpose of ensuring the person's compliance with the
    provision of the regulations to which the violation relates.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 19, s. 52
- [2019, c. 10, s. 177](#1154771-1191746){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1154771-1191746.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 467](#1410676-1414124){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410676-1414124.html"
  role="button"}
:::
